2. MMEL 30-01 (DDPG) Preparation - Wing Anti-Ice Valves Inoperative
(Figure 901)
A. General
(1) This task gives the maintenance steps which prepare the airplane for flight with the wing
anti-ice valves inoperative.
(2) The location of the wing anti-ice valve relative to the engine exhaust area prohibits personnel
from accessing the valve when the associated engine is running.
B. References
Reference Title
27-81-00-080-801 Leading Edge Flap and Slat Locks Removal (P/B 201)
27-81-00-480-801 Leading Edge Flap and Slat Locks Installation (P/B 201)
71-00-00-800-807-F00 Start the Engine Procedure (Selection) (P/B 201)
71-00-00-800-810-F00 Start the Engine Procedure (Engine Cross Bleed Start)
(P/B 201)
30-00-00
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
522 Left Wing - Slat No. 4
622 Right Wing - Slat No. 5
D. Access Panels
Number Name/Location
521AB Outboard Leading Edge Blowout Door
621AB Outboard Leading Edge Blowout Door
E. Prepare to Dispatch With a Wing Anti-Ice Valve Closed
SUBTASK 30-00-00-840-001
(1) Prepare to close the wing anti-ice valve:
WARNING
MAKE SURE THAT YOU DO THE PROCEDURE TO DEACTIVATE THE
LEADING EDGE SLATS. IF THE LEADING EDGE SLATS MOVE, INJURY
TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.
(a) Do this task: Leading Edge Flap and Slat Locks Installation, TASK 27-81-00-480-801.
(b) Open this circuit breaker and install safety tag:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
A 1 C00146 ANTI-ICE & RAIN WING ANTI-ICE VALVE
(c) For the left wing, open this access panel:
Number Name/Location
521AB Outboard Leading Edge Blowout Door
(d) For the right wing, open this access panel:
Number Name/Location
621AB Outboard Leading Edge Blowout Door
SUBTASK 30-00-00-020-001
(2) Disconnect the electrical connector from the wing anti-ice valve and stow the connector.
NOTE: The associated wing anti-ice VALVE OPEN light will illuminate bright.
SUBTASK 30-00-00-040-001
(3) Move the red lever on the wing anti-ice valve to the CLOSED position.
SUBTASK 30-00-00-840-002
(4) Return the airplane to its usual condition with a wing anti-ice valve locked closed:
(a) For the left wing, close this access panel:
Number Name/Location
521AB Outboard Leading Edge Blowout Door
(b) For the right wing, close this access panel:
Number Name/Location
621AB Outboard Leading Edge Blowout Door
30-00-00
(c) Remove the safety tag and close this circuit breaker:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
A 1 C00146 ANTI-ICE & RAIN WING ANTI-ICE VALVE
(d) Activate the LE slats. To activate them, do this task: Leading Edge Flap and Slat Locks
Removal, TASK 27-81-00-080-801.
SUBTASK 30-00-00-930-001
(5) Put an INOP OFF placard on the WING ANTI-ICE switch.
F . Prepare to Dispatch With the Left Wing Anti-Ice Valve Locked Open
SUBTASK 30-00-00-860-001
(1) To open the valve when APU bleed air will be used to start the engine, do these steps:
WARNING
MAKE SURE THAT YOU DO THE PROCEDURE TO DEACTIVATE THE
LEADING EDGE SLATS. IF THE LEADING EDGE SLATS MOVE, INJURY
TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.

connector.
NOTE: The associated wing anti-ice VALVE OPEN light will illuminate bright.
(e) Move the red lever on the left wing anti-ice valve to the CLOSED position.
WARNING
MAKE SURE THAT ALL PERSONNEL AND EQUIPMENT ARE CLEAR OF
THE RIGHT ENGINE DANGER AREA. THE ENGINE INTAKE AND
EXHAUST CAN CAUSE INJURY PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE
EQUIPMENT IN THE DANGER AREAS.
(f) Make sure that all personnel are cleared from the right engine danger area.
(g) For the right engine, do this task: Start the Engine Procedure (Selection),
TASK 71-00-00-800-807-F00.
(h) Close the isolation and APU bleed air valves to depressurize the left pneumatic duct.
(i) After the pneumatic duct is depressurized, move the wing anti-ice valve to the open
position.
(j) Close this access panel:
Number Name/Location
521AB Outboard Leading Edge Blowout Door

EXHAUST CAN CAUSE INJURY PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE
EQUIPMENT IN THE DANGER AREAS.

3. MMEL 30-01 (DDPG) Restoration - Wing Anti-Ice Valves Inoperative
A. General
(1) This task puts the airplane back to its usual condition after operation with the wing anti-ice
valves inoperative.
B. References
Reference Title
27-81-00-080-801 Leading Edge Flap and Slat Locks Removal (P/B 201)
27-81-00-480-801 Leading Edge Flap and Slat Locks Installation (P/B 201)

Zone Area

622 Right Wing - Slat No. 5
D. Access Panels
Number Name/Location
521AB Outboard Leading Edge Blowout Door
621AB Outboard Leading Edge Blowout Door
E. Reactivate the Wing Anti-Ice Valve
SUBTASK 30-00-00-860-005
(1) Open this circuit breaker and install safety tag:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
A 1 C00146 ANTI-ICE & RAIN WING ANTI-ICE VALVE
SUBTASK 30-00-00-860-006
(2) Set the WING ANTI-ICE switch on the anti-ice panel on the P5 overhead panel in the flight
compartment to OFF .
SUBTASK 30-00-00-860-007
WARNING
MAKE SURE YOU DO THE PROCEDURE TO DEACTIVATE THE LE SLATS.
THE LEADING EDGE SLATS CAN MOVE AND CAUSE INJURIES TO
PERSONS IF THEY ARE NOT DEACTIVATED.
(3) Do this task: Leading Edge Flap and Slat Locks Installation, TASK 27-81-00-480-801.
SUBTASK 30-00-00-010-001
(4) For the left wing, open this access panel:
Number Name/Location
521AB Outboard Leading Edge Blowout Door
SUBTASK 30-00-00-010-002
(5) For the right wing, open this access panel:
Number Name/Location
621AB Outboard Leading Edge Blowout Door
SUBTASK 30-00-00-420-001
(6) Connect the electrical connector to the wing anti-ice valve.
30-00-00
SUBTASK 30-00-00-860-008
(7) Remove the safety tag and close this circuit breaker:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
A 1 C00146 ANTI-ICE & RAIN WING ANTI-ICE VALVE
F . Wing Anti-Ice Valve Repair
SUBTASK 30-00-00-810-001
(1) Correct the fault.
(a) Find the fault code or description of the fault that occured.
(b) If you find a fault code, then do these steps:
1) Go to the Fault Code Index in the applicable chapter of the FIM and find the fault
code.
NOTE: The first two digits of the fault code is the FIM chapter.
2) Find the task number on the same line as the fault code.
3) Go to the task in the FIM and do the steps in the task.
(c) If you find a description of the fault, then do these steps:
1) Go to the Observed Fault List at the beginning of the FIM and find the best
description for the fault.
2) Find the task number on the same line as the fault description.
3) Go to the task in the FIM and do the steps in the task.
G. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition
SUBTASK 30-00-00-410-001
(1) For the left wing, close this access panel:
Number Name/Location
521AB Outboard Leading Edge Blowout Door
SUBTASK 30-00-00-410-002
(2) For the right wing, close this access panel:
Number Name/Location
621AB Outboard Leading Edge Blowout Door
SUBTASK 30-00-00-440-001
(3) Activate the LE slats. To activate them, do this task: Leading Edge Flap and Slat Locks
Removal, TASK 27-81-00-080-801.
SUBTASK 30-00-00-020-002
(4) If the fault was repaired, remove the INOP placard from the WING ANTI-ICE switch.

4. MMEL 30-02 (DDPG) Preparation - Wing Anti-Ice Valve Position Lights Inoperative
A. General
(1) This task gives the maintenance steps which prepare the airplane for flight with the wing
anti-ice valve position lights inoperative.
(2) This procedure tests the valve connected to the failed position light. If both position lights are
failed, both valves must be tested.
B. References
Reference Title
27-81-00-080-801 Leading Edge Flap and Slat Locks Removal (P/B 201)
27-81-00-480-801 Leading Edge Flap and Slat Locks Installation (P/B 201)

Zone Area
522 Left Wing - Slat No. 4
622 Right Wing - Slat No. 5
D. Access Panels
Number Name/Location
521AB Outboard Leading Edge Blowout Door
621AB Outboard Leading Edge Blowout Door
E. Prepare for the Procedure
SUBTASK 30-00-00-860-009
WARNING
MAKE SURE YOU DO THE PROCEDURE TO DEACTIVATE THE LE SLATS.
THE LEADING EDGE SLATS CAN MOVE AND CAUSE INJURIES TO
PERSONS IF THEY ARE NOT DEACTIVATED.
(1) Do this task: Leading Edge Flap and Slat Locks Installation, TASK 27-81-00-480-801.
SUBTASK 30-00-00-010-003
(2) For the left wing, open this access panel:
Number Name/Location
521AB Outboard Leading Edge Blowout Door
SUBTASK 30-00-00-010-004
(3) For the right wing, open this access panel:
Number Name/Location
621AB Outboard Leading Edge Blowout Door
F . Test the Wing Anti-Ice Valve
SUBTASK 30-00-00-710-001
(1) Put the WING ANTI-ICE switch in the ON position.
SUBTASK 30-00-00-710-002
(2) Observe the position indicator on the wing anti-ice valve and make sure the valve indicates
OPEN.
SUBTASK 30-00-00-710-003
(3) Put the WING ANTI-ICE switch in the OFF position.
30-00-00
SUBTASK 30-00-00-710-004
(4) Observe the position indicator on the wing anti-ice valve and make sure the valve indicates
CLOSED.
SUBTASK 30-00-00-930-004
(5) Attach an INOP placard to the applicable VALVE OPEN light.
G. Return the Airplane to Its Usual Condition
SUBTASK 30-00-00-010-005
(1) For the left wing, close this access panel:
Number Name/Location
521AB Outboard Leading Edge Blowout Door
SUBTASK 30-00-00-010-006
(2) For the right wing, close this access panel:
Number Name/Location
621AB Outboard Leading Edge Blowout Door
SUBTASK 30-00-00-440-002
(3) Do this task: Leading Edge Flap and Slat Locks Removal, TASK 27-81-00-080-801.

valve position lights inoperative.

SUBTASK 30-00-00-810-002

1) Go to the Fault Code Index in the applicable chapter of the FIM and find the fault
code.
NOTE: The first two digits of the fault code is the FIM chapter.

(c) If you find a description of the fault, then do these steps:

6. MMEL 30-03 (DDPG) Preparation - Engine and Nose Cowl Anti-Ice Valves Inoperative
(Figure 902, Figure 903)
A. General
(1) This task gives the maintenance steps which prepare the airplane for flight with the engine
anti-ice valves inoperative.
(2) The engine anti-ice valves can be deactivated by locking them closed or open.
B. References
Reference Title
71-11-02-010-801-F00 Open the Fan Cowl Panels (P/B 201)
71-11-02-410-801-F00 Close the Fan Cowl Panels (P/B 201)
78-31-00-010-801-F00 Open the Thrust Reverser (Selection) (P/B 201)
78-31-00-010-804-F00 Close the Thrust Reverser (Selection) (P/B 201)
C. Consumable Materials
Reference Description Specification
D00006 Compound - Antiseize, Pure Nickel Special -
Never-Seez NSBT-8N/-16N
D00010 Compound - Thread Antiseize, High
T emperature
MIL-PRF-907
D00667 Compound - Antiseize - Loctite C5-A Copper
Based Anti-Seize (Replaces FEL-PRO C5-A)
MIL-PRF-907
D50179 Compound - Lubricating (Lockrey Liqui-Moly
NV Thread Compound)
D50188 Compound - Anti-Seize, Loctite Silver Grade MIL-PRF-907
D. Location Zones
Zone Area
411 Engine 1 - Engine
421 Engine 2 - Engine

SUBTASK 30-00-00-010-008
(2) Do this task: Open the Thrust Reverser (Selection), TASK 78-31-00-010-801-F00.
SUBTASK 30-00-00-860-019
(3) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
A 6 C00148 ANTI-ICE & RAIN ENG 1 & WING CONT

30-00-00
(Continued)
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
B 6 C00149 ANTI-ICE & RAIN ENGINE 2 CONTROL
B 7 C01002 ANTI-ICE & RAIN ENG 2 COWL AI VALVE
SUBTASK 30-00-00-860-032
(4) Set each engine start lever in the CUTOFF position.
F . Put the Engine Anti-Ice Valve Inoperative Closed
SUBTASK 30-00-00-210-001
(1) To manually position the valve in the CLOSED position, do these steps:
(a) Loosen the retaining screw on the manual locking assembly.
(b) Move the hex shaft to the CLOSED desired position.
(c) Slide the locking assembly up the shaft into the recess in the cover plate.
(d) Tighten the retaining screw to 20 ±1 in-lb (2.26 ±0.11 N·m).
NOTE: Boeing recommends a 1/4 inch, 6 point, closed end spanner or socket to do this
step.
G. Put the Engine Anti-Ice Valve Inoperative Open
NOTE: Dispatching with an engine and nose cowl anti-ice valve inoperative open will cause the
associated COWL VALVE OPEN light to illuminate bright blue, on the P5-11 panel, and
associated Thermal Anti-Ice (TAI) indication to illuminate amber on the flight deck Common
Display System (CDS).
SUBTASK 30-00-00-210-002
(1) To manually position the valve in the OPEN position, do these steps:
(a) Loosen the retaining screw on the manual locking assembly.
(b) Move the hex shaft to the OPEN position.
(c) Slide the locking assembly up the shaft into the recess in the cover plate.
(d) Apply a small quantity of Liqui-Moly NV Thread Compound, D50179, to the threads of the
retaining screw.
(e) Tighten the retaining screw to 20 ±1 in-lb (2.26 ±0.11 N·m).
NOTE: Boeing recommends a 1/4 inch, 6 point, closed end spanner or socket to do this
step.
SUBTASK 30-00-00-020-006
(2) Disconnect, cap, and stow the DP1302 ELECTRICAL CONNECTOR [3] on the ANTI-ICE
PRESSURE SWITCH [4] (Figure 902).
SUBTASK 30-00-00-860-010
(3) Close the HIGH STAGE VALVE [5] from the 9th stage bleed air source (Figure 903).
NOTE: Only one high stage valve may be locked in the closed position for dispatch. Dispatch
with the opposite high stage valve inoperative under Master Minimum Equipment List
(MMEL) item 36-9 is not allowed.
(a) On the MANUAL OVERRIDE/POSITION INDICATOR [6], turn the manual override nut to
align the position indicator with the CLOSED position.
(b) Loosen the bolt.
(c) Push in the lock knob to lock the high stage valve closed.
30-00-00
(d) Apply Loctite LB 8150 anti-seize compound, D50188, or Loctite C5-A compound,
D00667, or compound, D00010, or Pure Nickel Special compound, D00006, to the
threads of the bolt.
(e) Tighten the bolt to 50 in-lb (5.65 N·m) - 55 in-lb (6.21 N·m) to hold the lock knob in its
position.
H. Secure the Engine Access Panels
SUBTASK 30-00-00-860-020
(1) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
A 6 C00148 ANTI-ICE & RAIN ENG 1 & WING CONT
A 7 C01001 ANTI-ICE & RAIN ENG 1 COWL AI VALVE
B 6 C00149 ANTI-ICE & RAIN ENGINE 2 CONTROL
B 7 C01002 ANTI-ICE & RAIN ENG 2 COWL AI VALVE
SUBTASK 30-00-00-010-010
(2) Do this task: Close the Thrust Reverser (Selection), TASK 78-31-00-010-804-F00.
SUBTASK 30-00-00-010-009
(3) Do this task: Close the Fan Cowl Panels, TASK 71-11-02-410-801-F00.
I. Put INOP Placards on the Affected Switch and Light
SUBTASK 30-00-00-930-005
(1) Put an INOP LOCKED OPEN or INOP LOCKED CLOSED placard on the affected ENG
ANTI-ICE switch.

30-00-00

retaining screw.

NOTE: Only one high stage valve may be locked in the closed position for dispatch. Dispatch
with the opposite high stage valve inoperative under MMEL item 36-9 is not allowed.
(a) On the MANUAL OVERRIDE/POSITION INDICATOR [6], turn the manual override nut to
align the position indicator with the CLOSED position.
(b) Loosen the bolt.
(c) Push in the lock knob to lock the high stage valve closed.
(d) Apply Loctite LB 8150 anti-seize compound, D50188, or Loctite C5-A compound,
D00667, or compound, D00010, or Pure Nickel Special compound, D00006 to the thread
of the bolt.

position.

3. Wing Anti-Icing Duct - Leak Test
(Figure 501)
A. General
(1) This test checks for leaks in the wing anti-icing ducts in the fixed leading edge of the wings,
and in the telescoping ducts. The check includes a visual inspection of the ducts and
connectors. The check also includes feeling for leaks at the clamp connectors and duct
sleeves.
B. References
Reference Title

(P/B 201)

F . Procedure
SUBTASK 30-11-00-210-002
(1) Do a visual check of the wing thermal anti-icing duct and fittings.
(a) Inspect for holes, cracks, and loose components.
SUBTASK 30-11-00-840-001
(2) Supply pneumatic pressure for the ducts.
30-11-00
(a) To use the APU to pressurize the TAI duct, do this task: Supply Pressure to the
Pneumatic System with the APU, TASK 36-00-00-860-803.
(b) To use an external air supply to pressurize the TAI duct, do this task: Supply Pressure to
the Pneumatic System with an External Ground Air Source, TASK 36-00-00-860-802.
(c) Put the BLEED 1 and 2 switches on the air conditioning panel to the OFF position.
(d) Put the L PACK and R PACK switches on the air conditioning panel to the OFF position.
(e) Make sure the ISOLATION VALVE switch is in the OPEN or the AUTO position.
SUBTASK 30-11-00-860-011
CAUTION
DO NOT KEEP THE WING TAI VALVE OPEN FOR MORE THAN 30 SECONDS
WHEN THE PNEUMATIC SYSTEM IS PRESSURIZED. IF IT IS OPEN FOR
MORE THAN 30 SECONDS, THE HOT AIR CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE
WING LEADING EDGE.
(3) Put the WING ANTI-ICE switch to the ON position.
SUBTASK 30-11-00-200-001
(4) Do this check for leaks from the TAI duct:
WARNING
DO NOT TOUCH THE TAI DUCT . THE DUCT CAN GET HOT AND
INJURIES TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN
OCCUR.
(a) Move your hand near the TAI duct to feel for leaks.
1) Some leakage along duct seals and joints is satisfactory.
2) Leaks which can be felt 12 in. (305 mm) or more away are not satisfactory.
SUBTASK 30-11-00-200-002
WARNING
DO NOT TOUCH THE TAI DUCT . THE DUCT CAN GET HOT AND INJURIES
TO PERSONNEL AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.
(5) Do this check for leaks around the connection between the thermal switch and the duct boss:
(a) Move your hand near the thermal switch and the duct boss to feel for leaks.
1) Some leakage along duct seals and joints is satisfactory.
2) Leaks which can be felt 12 in. (305 mm) or more away are not satisfactory.
SUBTASK 30-11-00-720-006
(6) Put the WING ANTI-ICE switch to the OFF position.

SUBTASK 30-11-11-040-002
WARNING
DO THE DEACTIVATION PROCEDURE FOR THE LEADING EDGE SLATS.
THE SLATS RETRACT QUICKLY . THIS CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO
PERSONNEL OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(1) Make sure that the leading edge slats are deactivated (TASK 27-81-00-040-801).
SUBTASK 30-11-11-860-012
(2) Make sure that this circuit breaker is open and has safety tag:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
A 1 C00146 ANTI-ICE & RAIN WING ANTI-ICE VALVE
I. Wing Thermal Anti-Icing Shutoff Valve Installation
SUBTASK 30-11-11-420-001
(1) Put the valve [6] in its position.
SUBTASK 30-11-11-420-002
(2) Install the coupling [3] and coupling [4].
(a) Tighten the coupling [3] to 55 in-lb (6.2 N·m) - 60 in-lb (6.8 N·m).
1) Make sure that the loop bands do not touch.
2) Make sure that the clearance between the retainers is 0.050 in. (1.270 mm) -
0.410 in. (10.414 mm).
(b) Tighten the coupling [4] to 40 in-lb (4.5 N·m) - 50 in-lb (5.6 N·m).
1) Make sure that the loop bands do not touch.
2) Make sure that the clearance between the retainers is 0.050 in. (1.270 mm) -
0.310 in. (7.874 mm).
SUBTASK 30-11-11-420-004
(3) Do the following to connect the jumper [10]:
(a) Clean the bonding surfaces of the jumper [10], washer [8], washer [11], and valve [6] with
alcohol, B00130, or equivalent (SWPM 20-20-00, Section 2E, Cleaning of Faying
Surfaces).
1) Use Cleaning Procedure 5 to clean the surfaces.
30-11-11
(b) Install the screw [12], washer [8], washer [11], and nut [7].
(c) Measure the electrical bonding resistance between the valve [6] and airplane structure
(SWPM 20-20-00).
1) Use an intrinsically safe approved bonding meter, COM-1550.
2) Make sure that the electrical bonding resistance is 0.0025 ohms (2.5 milliohms) or
less.
SUBTASK 30-11-11-420-005
(4) Connect the electrical connector [1].

(3) Open the applicable wing access panels:
Number Name/Location
521AB Outboard Leading Edge Blowout Door
621AB Outboard Leading Edge Blowout Door
F . Procedure
SUBTASK 30-11-11-860-006
(1) Press and release the L VALVE OPEN and R VALVE OPEN light switches on the P5-11 panel.
(a) Make sure each light comes on then goes off.
SUBTASK 30-11-11-860-007
(2) Make sure the TAI valves open correctly.
(a) Put the WING ANTI-ICE switch in the ON position.
(b) Make sure the VALVE OPEN lights come on brightly for 1 to 6 seconds.
30-11-11
(c) Make sure the VALVE OPEN lights are on dimly after 6 seconds.
(d) Make sure the wing TAI valves are open.
SUBTASK 30-11-11-720-001
(3) Test the TAI valve indication for the left wing:
(a) Open this circuit breaker:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
A 1 C00146 ANTI-ICE & RAIN WING ANTI-ICE VALVE
(b) Disconnect the electrical connector from the left wing TAI valve.
(c) Make sure the L VALVE OPEN light is on brightly and continuously.
(d) Reconnect the electrical connector to the TAI valve.
(e) Close this circuit breaker:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
A 1 C00146 ANTI-ICE & RAIN WING ANTI-ICE VALVE
(f) Make sure the L VALVE OPEN is on dimly and continuously.
SUBTASK 30-11-11-720-002
(4) Test the TAI valve indication for the right wing:
(a) Open this circuit breaker:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
A 1 C00146 ANTI-ICE & RAIN WING ANTI-ICE VALVE
(b) Disconnect the electrical connector from the right wing TAI valve.
(c) Make sure the R VALVE OPEN light is on brightly and continuously.
(d) Reconnect the electrical connector to the TAI valve.
(e) Close this circuit breaker:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
A 1 C00146 ANTI-ICE & RAIN WING ANTI-ICE VALVE
(f) Make sure the R VALVE OPEN is on dimly and continuously.
SUBTASK 30-11-11-860-008
(5) Make sure the TAI valves close correctly.
(a) Put the WING ANTI-ICE switch in the OFF position.
(b) Make sure the VALVE OPEN lights come on brightly for 1 to 6 seconds.
(c) Make sure the VALVE OPEN lights go off after 6 seconds.
(d) Make sure the wing TAI valves are closed.
30-11-11

621AB Outboard Leading Edge Blowout Door

G. Ground Wing TAI Solenoid Valve Test
SUBTASK 30-11-12-480-001
(1) Do these steps to connect the test equipment to the engine:
(a) Disconnect the bleed air supply line [4] at the inlet tee at the supply pressure sense
line [3] on the left of the engine.
(b) Connect the 0-100 PSIG nitrogen source, STD-1115, pressure regulator, STD-1198, and
test line at the tee to the supply pressure sense line [3].
SUBTASK 30-11-12-710-002
(2) Do this test of the ground wing TAI solenoid valve [1]:
(a) Slowly increase the supply pressure to between 25 psig (1.76 kg/cm
2) and 30 psig
(2.11 kg/cm2).
(b) Make sure that the precooler control valve [2] goes closed.
(c) Put the WING ANTI-ICE switch on the P5-11 engine and wing anti-ice control panel in the
ON position.
(d) Make sure that these actions occur:
1) Make sure that the precooler control valve [2] goes fully open, or within 30 degrees
from fully open.
SUBTASK 30-11-12-720-002
(3) Do this check of the air mode transition:
WARNING
OBEY THE PROCEDURE THAT PUTS THE AIRPLANE IN THE AIR MODE.
IF YOU DO THE PROCEDURE INCORRECTLY , INJURIES TO
PERSONNEL, AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.
(a) Do this task: Prepare to Put the Airplane in the Air Mode, TASK 32-09-00-840-801.
(b) Do this task: Put the Airplane in the Air Mode, TASK 32-09-00-860-801.
1) Make sure that the precooler control valve [2] goes closed.
(c) Make sure that the WING ANTI-ICE switch goes to the OFF position.
(d) Put the WING ANTI-ICE switch to the ON position.
30-11-12
1) Make sure that the precooler control valve [2] remains closed.
(e) Put the WING ANTI-ICE switch in the OFF position.
(f) Decrease the supply pressure to 0 psig.
(g) Return the airplane to the ground mode, do this task: Return the Airplane to the Ground
Mode, TASK 32-09-00-860-802.
SUBTASK 30-11-12-080-003
(4) Remove the supply pressure (Ps) test equipment and reinstall the bleed air supply line [4] to
the supply pressure sense line [3] inlet tee as follows:
(a) Remove the 0-100 PSIG nitrogen source, STD-1115, pressure regulator, STD-1198, and
test line from the supply pressure sense line [3].
(b) Connect the bleed air supply line [4] to the inlet tee of the supply pressure sense line [3]
(View B, Figure 501).
1) Apply a light coat of compound, D00010, or Pure Nickel Special compound,
D00006, on the threads of all fittings when connecting the sense lines.
CAUTION
USE TWO WRENCHES TO LOOSEN OR TIGHTEN THE FITTING. IF YOU
TWIST THE TUBE OR FITTING, YOU CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THEM.
(c) Tighten the coupling nuts for the bleed air supply line [4] (Ps) to 133.0 in-lb (15.0 N·m) -
147.0 in-lb (17 N·m).
CAUTION
USE TWO WRENCHES TO LOOSEN OR TIGHTEN THE FITTING. IF YOU
TWIST THE TUBE OR FITTING, YOU CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THEM.
(d) Back off the coupling nuts to decrease the torque.
CAUTION
USE TWO WRENCHES TO LOOSEN OR TIGHTEN THE FITTING. IF YOU
TWIST THE TUBE OR FITTING, YOU CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THEM.
(e) Tighten the coupling nuts for the bleed air supply line [4] (Ps) to 133.0 in-lb (15.0 N·m) -
147.0 in-lb (17 N·m).
SUBTASK 30-11-12-780-001
(5) Do this task: Supply Pressure Upstream of the PRSOV, TASK 36-00-00-860-805.
SUBTASK 30-11-12-200-001
(6) Inspect and check for leaks at the bleed air supply line [4] at the inlet tee at the supply
pressure sense line [3] on the left of the engine (Figure 501).
NOTE: No leakage is permitted at the sense line connections.
SUBTASK 30-11-12-780-002
(7) Do this task: Remove Pressure from the Pneumatic System, TASK 36-00-00-860-806.

(2) Turn the inner telescoping duct [1] 90 degrees and lower the duct from the wing.
SUBTASK 30-11-31-020-022
(3) Remove the O-ring [11], O-rings [12], or O-ring [13] from the swivel joint of the inner
telescoping duct [1].
SUBTASK 30-11-31-020-023
(4) Discard the O-ring [11], O-rings [12], and O-ring [13].
SUBTASK 30-11-31-020-005
(5) Pull the inner telescoping duct [1] from the outer telescoping duct [26].
SUBTASK 30-11-31-020-004
(6) Disassemble the telescoping duct seal.
(a) Remove the lockwire [3].
(b) Loosen the cap nut [4].
30-11-31
(c) Remove the cap nut [4], back up rings [2], ring [7], seal [8], and retainer [9].

H. Wing Anti-Ice Telescoping Duct Seal Installation
SUBTASK 30-11-31-860-003
WARNING
MAKE SURE THAT PERSONS AND EQUIPMENT ARE CLEAR OF THE
LEADING EDGE SLATS. THE LEADING EDGE SLATS CAN MOVE QUICKLY .
THIS CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(1) If the actuator locks are not installed, do this task: Leading Edge Flap and Slat Locks
Installation, TASK 27-81-00-480-801.
SUBTASK 30-11-31-420-001
(2) Assemble the telescoping duct seal.
(a) Put grease, D00013, on the inside diameter of the outer duct assembly.
(b) Install the retainer [9], back up rings [2], seal [8], ring [7], and cap nut [4] on the inner
telescoping duct [1] (Figure 401).
1) It is necessary to install seal [8] with the larger cutout facing to the outer telescoping
duct [26] and the small dimple facing to cap nut [4] (View A-A, Figure 401).
(c) Tighten the cap nut [4] to 250.0 in-lb (28.2 N·m) - 370.0 in-lb (41.8 N·m).
(d) Install the lockwire [3] on the cap nut [4] (TASK 20-10-44-400-801).
SUBTASK 30-11-31-420-012
(3) Install the new O-ring [11], new O-ring [12], or new O-ring [13], as necessary, on the swivel
joint of the inner telescoping duct [1].
(a) If it is necessary use silicone compound, D00254, on the O-rings to make installation
easier.
SUBTASK 30-11-31-420-013
(4) To install the inner telescoping duct [1], do these steps:
(a) Put the bearing [10] and bearing [14] on the ends of the inner telescoping duct [1].
(b) Put the inner telescoping duct [1] into position.
(c) Install the bolts [16] and washers [15].
1) Do not tighten the bolts [16] until after the supply duct [6] is installed.
SUBTASK 30-11-31-420-014
(5) To install the supply duct [6], do these steps:
(a) Clean the mating surfaces of the supply duct [6] tab and support assembly (SWPM
20-20-00).
1) Use cotton wiper, G00034, made moist with alcohol, B00130.
2) Immediately dry the surfaces with a new cotton wiper, G00034.
(b) Apply a bead of sealant, A50231, along the top edge of the support assembly and
bearings.
(c) Put the supply duct [6] into position.
(d) Install the bolts [17], bolts [18], and washers [15].
(e) Tighten the bolts [16], bolts [17], and bolts [18].
(f) Measure the electrical bonding resistance between the supply duct [6] tab and support
assembly (SWPM 20-20-00).
1) Use an intrinsically safe approved bonding meter, COM-1550.
30-11-31
2) Make sure that the bonding resistance is 0.00070 ohms (0.70 milliohms) or less.
(g) Fillet seal the supply duct [6] tab with sealant, A50231.
(h) Position the clamps [5] so that there is a clearance of at least 0.2 in. (5.1 mm) from any
adjacent structure.
1) Tighten the clamps [5] (TASK 20-50-11-910-801).
a) Make sure that the loop bands do not touch.
b) Make sure that the clearance between the retainers is 0.050 in. (1.270 mm) -
0.410 in. (10.414 mm).

1) Open these access panels:
Number Name/Location

(a) Remove the cover plate [22].
SUBTASK 30-11-31-020-010

(a) Remove the retaining bolts [32].
(b) Remove the washers [33], sleeves [34], springs [35], spacers [36], and spring

the spray duct bearings [19], can be removed.

G. Inner Telescoping Duct Removal
SUBTASK 30-11-31-020-016
WARNING
DO NOT LET OBJECTS GET IN THE HOUSING ASSEMBLY OF THE SLAT
TRACK. THIS WILL HELP PREVENT A PUNCTURE OF THE HOUSING
ASSEMBLY THAT COULD CAUSE A FUEL LEAK. THE FUEL LEAK COULD
CAUSE A FIRE AND POSSIBLE DEATH OR INJURY TO PERSONNEL.
(1) Do these steps to remove the supply ducts [6], bearing [10], and bearing [14]:
(a) Remove the bolt [16], bolt [17], bolt [18], and washers [15].
(b) Disconnect the clamp [5] from one side of the supply duct [6].
1) Remove the section from the supply ducts [6].
(c) Remove the bearing [10] and bearing [14].
SUBTASK 30-11-31-020-017
(2) Turn the inner telescoping duct [1] 90 degrees and lower the duct from the wing.
SUBTASK 30-11-31-020-018
(3) Remove the O-ring [11], O-rings [12], and O-ring [13] from the swivel joint of the inner
telescoping duct [1].
SUBTASK 30-11-31-020-019
(4) Discard the O-ring [11], O-rings [12] and O-ring [13].

H. Inner Telescoping Duct Installation
SUBTASK 30-11-31-420-015
(1) Install the O-ring [11], O-rings [12], and O-ring [13], as necessary, on the swivel joint of the
inner telescoping duct [1].
(a) If it is necessary, use silicone compound, D00254, on the O-rings to make installation
easier.
SUBTASK 30-11-31-420-016
(2) To install the inner telescoping duct [1], do these steps:
(a) Put the bearing [10] and bearing [14] on the ends of the inner telescoping duct [1].
(b) Put the inner telescoping duct [1] into position.
(c) Install the bolts [16] and washers [15].
1) Do not tighten the bolts until after the supply duct is installed.
SUBTASK 30-11-31-420-017
(3) To install the supply duct [6], do these steps:
(a) Clean the mating surfaces of the supply duct [6] tab and support assembly (SWPM
20-20-00).
1) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, made moist with alcohol, B00130.
2) Immediately dry the surfaces with a new cotton wiper, G00034.
(b) Apply a bead of sealant, A50231, along the top edge of the support assembly and
bearings.
(c) Put the supply duct [6] into position.
(d) Install the bolts [17], bolts [18], and washers [15].
(e) Tighten the bolts [16], bolts [17], and bolts [18].
(f) Measure the electrical bonding resistance between the supply duct [6] tab and the
support assembly (SWPM 20-20-00).
1) Use an intrinsically safe approved bonding meter, COM-1550.
2) Make sure that the bonding resistance is 0.00070 ohms (0.70 milliohms) or less.
(g) Fillet seal the supply duct [6] tab with sealant, A50231.
(h) Position the clamps [5] so that there is a clearance of at least 0.2 in. (5.1 mm) from any
adjacent structure.
1) Tighten the clamps [5] (TASK 20-50-11-910-801).
a) Make sure that the loop bands do not touch.
b) Make sure that the clearance between the retainers is 0.050 in. (1.270 mm) -
0.410 in. (10.414 mm).
I. Outer Telescoping Duct Installation
SUBTASK 30-11-31-430-006
(1) Put the spray duct bearings [19] on each end of the outer telescoping duct [26].
SUBTASK 30-11-31-430-007
(2) Put the outer telescoping duct [26] into the support brackets [30].
SUBTASK 30-11-31-430-008
(3) Do this to install spray duct [27].
(a) Clean the mating surfaces of the spray duct [27] flange, washers [43], and rib end [31].
30-11-31
1) Use cotton wiper, G00034, made moist with alcohol, B00130.
2) Immediately dry the surfaces with a new cotton wiper, G00034.
(b) Install the retaining bolts [25], washers [43], and spray duct [27].
SUBTASK 30-11-31-430-009
(4) Measure the electrical bonding resistance between the spray duct [27] flange and the rib
end [31] (SWPM 20-20-00).
(a) Use an intrinsically safe approved bonding meter, COM-1550.
(b) Make sure that the bonding resistance is 0.00070 ohms (0.70 milliohms) or less.
SUBTASK 30-11-31-420-010
(5) Fillet seal the spray duct [27] flange with Dow Corning 93-006-1 RF sealant, A00900, or
sealant, A00160.
SUBTASK 30-11-31-420-006
(6) Do these steps to install the blade seal [24]:
(a) Connect the washer [33], washers [44], sleeve [34], spring [35], spacer [36], and spring
retainer [37] to the blade seal [24].
(b) Connect the washers [33], sleeves [34], springs [35], spacers [36], and spring
retainers [37] to the blade seal [24] (2 locations).
(c) Install the retaining bolts [32] to attach the blade seal [24].
SUBTASK 30-11-31-410-002
(7) Do this step to install the access door:
(a) Install the cover plate [22].
SUBTASK 30-11-31-410-009
(8) Do these steps to install the inner skin panel of the outer slat:
(a) Install the applicable cover panel [21]:
1) Open these access panels:
Number Name/Location
522CB Slat No. 4 - Cove Skin
523CB Slat No. 3 - Cove Skin
524CB Slat No. 2 - Cove Skin
622CB Slat No. 5 - Cove Skin
623CB Slat No. 6 - Cove Skin
624CB Slat No. 7 - Cove Skin
(b) Install the seal [20].
(c) Install the backup plates [28].
1) Apply compound, C00528, to the hex bolt on the lower backup plate [28].
J. Wing Anit-Icing Duct Leak Test
SUBTASK 30-11-31-790-002
(1) Do this task: Wing Anti-Icing Duct - Leak Test, TASK 30-11-00-790-801.
K. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition
SUBTASK 30-11-31-410-011
(1) Install these panels, do this task: Leading Edge Access Panel Installation,
TASK 57-41-02-400-801.
30-11-31
(a) For the left wing, close these access panel:
Number Name/Location
521DB Lower Leading Edge Access Panel
521KB Lower Leading Edge Access Panel
521RB Lower Leading Edge Access Panel
(b) For the right wing, close these access panel:
Number Name/Location
621DB Lower Leading Edge Access Panel
621JB Lower Leading Edge Access Panel
621QB Lower Leading Edge Access Panel
SUBTASK 30-11-31-420-005
(2) Clean the mating surfaces of the ground strap [40] and bracket [39].
(a) Use cotton wiper, G00034, made moist with alcohol, B00130.
(b) Immediately dry the surfaces with a new cotton wiper, G00034.
SUBTASK 30-11-31-420-011
(3) Install the bolts [38], ground strap [40], washers [41], and nuts [42] that hold the bracket [39] to
the outer telescoping duct [26].
(a) Adjust the vertical position of the bracket [39] to make sure that it is flush with the other
access panels.
(b) Tighten the bolts [38] after the vertical position of the bracket [39] is set.
(c) Do a bonding check for the ground strap [40].
1) Measure the electrical bonding resistance between door and duct (SWPM
20-20-00).
a) Use and intrinsically safe approved bonding meter, COM-1550.
b) Make sure that the bonding resistance is 0.010 ohms (10 milliohms) or less.
(d) Apply sealant, A50231, to the terminal that was removed.
SUBTASK 30-11-31-860-008
(4) Do this task: Leading Edge Flap and Slat Locks Removal, TASK 27-81-00-080-801.
SUBTASK 30-11-31-410-004
(5) Adjust the telescoping duct door [2], if needed, to get equal clearances on the inboard and
outboard sides, and equal clearances and on the forward and aft sides (Figure 402).

1. Wing Anti-Ice Telescoping Duct Check
A. General
(1) This procedure has the wear limits for the inner telescoping duct of the anti-ice telescopic
ducts. These ducts are installed on the leading edges for slats 2 through 7.
B. References
Reference Title
27-81-00-080-801 Leading Edge Flap and Slat Locks Removal (P/B 201)
27-81-00-480-801 Leading Edge Flap and Slat Locks Installation (P/B 201)

SOPM 20-30-03 General Cleaning Procedures
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
520 Subzone - Left Wing: Leading Edge, Fwd of Front Spar, Outboard of Strut and
Nacelle Gap Cover Area
620 Subzone - Right Wing: Leading Edge, Forward of Front Spar, Outboard of Nacelle
Strut
D. Procedure
SUBTASK 30-11-31-860-009
WARNING
MAKE SURE THAT ALL PERSONNEL AND EQUIPMENT ARE AWAY FROM
THE SLATS. THE SLATS MOVE QUICKLY . INJURIES TO PERSONNEL AND
DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.
(1) Do this task to extend the leading edge slats and lock them out: Leading Edge Flap and Slat
Locks Installation, TASK 27-81-00-480-801.
SUBTASK 30-11-31-211-001
(2) Examine the inner telescoping duct [1] for dents, damage, or wear. If the inner telescoping
duct [1] is coated, use the Table 601 for coating wear limits.
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH TEFLON COATED WING THERMAL ANTI-ICE DUCT
Table 601/30-11-31-993-801 TEFLON COATING WEAR LIMIT CHART
Max allowable wear damage of
coating for Area I
Max allowable wear damage of coating for
Area II

Coating total missing SLAT 3 (6), 11.0 in 2 (7096.8 mm 2) Unlimited
Coating total missing SLAT 2 (7), 12.5 in 2 (8064.5 mm 2) Unlimited
Max length of radial damage 1.00 in. (25.40 mm) Unlimited
Max length of longitudinal damage Unlimited Unlimited
30-11-31
RYR ALL; AIRPLANES WITH TEFLON COATED WING THERMAL ANTI-ICE DUCT (Continued)
(a) If the damage to the coating is more than the wear limits, remove the coating from the
inner telescoping duct [1] (SOPM 20-30-03).
RYR ALL
(b) If dents or damage is found on the base metal, replace the duct.
1) Wing Anti-Ice T elescoping Duct Removal, TASK 30-11-31-000-802
2) Wing Anti-Ice T elescoping Duct Installation, TASK 30-11-31-400-802
E. Put the airplane back to its usual condition
SUBTASK 30-11-31-860-010
(1) Do this task to unlock the leading edge slats and retract them: Leading Edge Flap and Slat
Locks Removal, TASK 27-81-00-080-801.

2. Engine and Wing Anti-Ice Panel Removal
(Figure 401)
A. General
(1) This task gives instructions to remove the engine and wing anti-ice panel.
B. References
Reference Title
20-40-12-000-802 ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Removal (P/B 201)
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
212 Flight Compartment - Right
D. Prepare for the Removal
SUBTASK 30-11-41-860-012
(1) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
A 1 C00146 ANTI-ICE & RAIN WING ANTI-ICE VALVE
A 6 C00148 ANTI-ICE & RAIN ENG 1 & WING CONT
A 7 C01001 ANTI-ICE & RAIN ENG 1 COWL AI VALVE
B 6 C00149 ANTI-ICE & RAIN ENGINE 2 CONTROL
B 7 C01002 ANTI-ICE & RAIN ENG 2 COWL AI VALVE
SUBTASK 30-11-41-010-002
(2) Open the P5 overhead panel.
(a) Loosen the quarter-turn fasteners on the bottom corners of the P5 overhead panel.
WARNING
HOLD THE PANEL TIGHTLY BEFORE YOU RELEASE THE LATCH. THE
PANEL IS HEAVY . INJURIES CAN OCCUR IF IT HITS YOU.
(b) Release the panel safety latch.
SUBTASK 30-11-41-910-001
WARNING
DO NOT TOUCH THE CONNECTOR PINS, OR OTHER CONDUCTORS. IF
YOU TOUCH THESE CONDUCTORS, ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE CAN
CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE COMPONENTS.
(3) Do this task: ESDS Handling for Metal Encased Unit Removal, TASK 20-40-12-000-802.
30-11-41
E. Engine and Wing Anti-Ice Panel Removal
SUBTASK 30-11-41-020-002
WARNING
THERE ARE HIGH VOLTAGES IN THE P5 OVERHEAD PANEL. MAKE SURE
THAT YOU DO NOT TOUCH OR SHORT EXPOSED TERMINALS. INJURY TO
PERSONNEL OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.
(1) Remove the connectors [3] from the engine and wing anti-ice control module [1].
SUBTASK 30-11-41-020-001
(2) Remove the engine and wing anti-ice control module [1]:
(a) Release the quarter-turn fasteners [2] that attach the engine and wing anti-ice control
module [1] to the P5 overhead panel.
(b) Remove the engine and wing anti-ice control module [1].
SUBTASK 30-11-41-410-003
(3) If it is necessary, close the P5 overhead panel.
WARNING
DO NOT TOUCH THE CONNECTOR PINS, OR OTHER CONDUCTORS.
IF YOU TOUCH THESE CONDUCTORS, ELECTROSTATIC DISCHARGE
CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE COMPONENTS.
(a) Raise the P5 overhead panel.
(b) Make sure that the safety latch is in the proper position.
(c) Tighten the quarter-turn fasteners on the bottom corners of the P5 overhead panel.

SUBTASK 30-11-41-860-004

SUBTASK 30-11-41-860-005
CAUTION

SECONDS. DAMAGE TO THE AIRCRAFT CAN OCCUR.
(2) Make sure that the engine and wing anti-ice panel commands the anti-icing valves open:

(c) Make sure that the VALVE OPEN lights are on dimly after 6 seconds.

(c) Make sure that the VALVE OPEN lights are on dimly after 6 seconds.

(a) Put the WING ANTI-ICE switch in the OFF position.

(c) Make sure that the VALVE OPEN lights go off after 6 seconds.

(2) Manually open the Pressure Regulating and Shutoff Valve (PRSOV) valve on each engine:
30-11-41
WARNING
DO THESE SPECIFIED TASKS IN THE CORRECT SEQUENCE BEFORE

DO THE DEACTIVATION PROCEDURES FOR THE LEADING EDGE AND

(d) Make sure that the PRSOV stays in the open position.
NOTE: The PRSOV is spring-loaded to the closed position. If there is pressure supplied
and the PRSOV does not stay open, there may be a leakage in the PRSOV or in
the sense line(s).

2) Make sure that the COWL VALVE OPEN light goes dimly after 6 seconds.

SUBTASK 30-21-00-910-001
CAUTION
DO NOT KEEP THE COWL TAI VALVE OPEN FOR MORE THAN THE
SPECIFIED TIME WHEN THE TEMPERATURE IS MORE THAN THE
SPECIFIED LIMIT . DAMAGE TO THE INLET COWL CAN OCCUR.
(1) If the outside air temperature is more than 50°F (10°C), operate the engine cowl anti-icing
system for 30 seconds or less.
NOTE: The temperature limit of 50°F (10°C) is only applicable to the engine cowl anti-ice
valve installation/system test.
30-21-00
SUBTASK 30-21-00-910-002
(2) There is no time limit for operation of the engine cowl anti-icing system when the temperature
is at or less than 50°F (10°C).
SUBTASK 30-21-00-840-002
(3) Do one of the following steps to provide pneumatic pressure for the ducts:
(a) Use the engines to pressurize the TAI duct, do this task: Start the Engine Procedure
(Selection), TASK 71-00-00-800-807-F00.
(b) Use the Auxiliary Power Unit (APU) to pressurize the TAI duct, do this task: Supply
Pressure to the Pneumatic System with the APU, TASK 36-00-00-860-803.
(c) Use an external air supply to pressurize the TAI duct, do this task: Supply Pressure to the
Pneumatic System with an External Ground Air Source, TASK 36-00-00-860-802.
(d) If you use the APU, external air, or a single engine to pressurize the TAI duct, then do
these steps:
1) Put the BLEED 1 and 2 switches on the air conditioning panel in the OFF position.
2) Put the L PACK and R PACK switches on the air conditioning panel in the OFF
position.
3) Make sure that the ISOLATION VALVE switch is in the OPEN or AUTO position.
4) If you use the APU or external air to pressurize the TAI duct, then you must
manually open thePressure Regulating and Shutoff Valve (PRSOV) valves.
5) If you use the a single engine to pressurize the TAI duct, then you must manually
open the PRSOV valve on the non-running engine.
6) Open a PRSOV valve, do these steps on the applicable engine or engines:
WARNING
DO THESE SPECIFIED TASKS IN THE CORRECT SEQUENCE
BEFORE YOU OPEN THE THRUST REVERSERS: RETRACT
THE LEADING EDGE, DO THE DEACTIVATION PROCEDURES
FOR THE LEADING EDGE AND THE THRUST REVERSER (FOR
GROUND MAINTENANCE), AND OPEN THE FAN COWL
PANELS. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY THE ABOVE SEQUENCE,
INJURIES TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN
OCCUR.
a) Open the left thrust reverser, do this task: Open the Thrust Reverser
(Selection), TASK 78-31-00-010-801-F00.
b) Put the applicable engine BLEED switch, on the P5-10 forward overhead
panel, to the ON position.
c) Use a wrench on the manual override nut for the PRSOV to put it to the OPEN
position.
d) Make sure that the PRSOV stays in the OPEN position.
NOTE: The PRSOV is spring-loaded to the closed position. If there is
pressure supplied and the PRSOV does not stay open, there may be
a leakage in the PRSOV or in the sense line(s).
SUBTASK 30-21-00-860-003

(a) Push and release the COWL VALVE OPEN and COWL ANTI-ICE light switches on the
P5-11 panel.
30-21-00
(b) Make sure that each light comes on then goes off.
SUBTASK 30-21-00-860-004
(5) Make sure that the engine and wing anti-ice panel commands the cowl TAI valves open.
NOTE: If the COWL VALVE OPEN lights remain illuminated bright with engines at IDLE,
position APU BLEED air switch to OFF and increase thrust slightly (up to a maximum
of 30% N1).
(a) Put the ENG ANTI-ICE 1 switch in the ON position.
1) Make sure that the left COWL VALVE OPEN light comes on brightly within 6
seconds.
2) Make sure that the COWL VALVE OPEN light becomes dim after it comes on
brightly.
3) Make sure that the green TAI indication shows on the Common Display System
(CDS) display above the left N1 speed display.
(b) Put the ENG ANTI-ICE 2 switch in the ON position.
1) Make sure that the right COWL VALVE OPEN light comes on brightly within 6
seconds.
2) Make sure that the COWL VALVE OPEN light becomes dim after it comes on
brightly.
3) Make sure that the green TAI indication shows on the CDS display above the right
N1 speed display.
SUBTASK 30-21-00-860-005

(a) Put the ENG ANTI-ICE 1 switch in the OFF position.
1) Make sure that the left COWL VALVE OPEN light comes on brightly within 3
seconds.
2) Make sure that the COWL VALVE OPEN light then goes off.
(b) Put the ENG ANTI-ICE 2 switch in the OFF position.
1) Make sure that the right COWL VALVE OPEN light comes on brightly within 3
seconds.
2) Make sure that the COWL VALVE OPEN light then goes off.
SUBTASK 30-21-00-860-010
(7) Allow the PRSOV to return to the CLOSED position by spring force only.
NOTE: It is allowable to use slight hand pressure on the wrench at the end of the stroke to
assist the valve to the full closed position.
The valve is designed to have air pressure to assist the valve closure to overcome the
resistance of the valve's butterfly seal.
(a) If the PRSOV does not move smooth or return to the CLOSED position on the third cycle,
replace the valve, do these tasks:
• PRSOV Removal, TASK 36-11-04-000-801,
• PRSOV Installation, TASK 36-11-04-400-801.

30-21-00
(a) Do this task: Stop the Engine Procedure (Usual Engine Stop),

SUBTASK 30-21-00-010-001
(11) For the right fan cowl panel, do this task: Open the Fan Cowl Panels,
TASK 71-11-02-010-801-F00.
SUBTASK 30-21-00-720-004
(12) Do this test of the left cowl TAI duct overpressure detection circuit:
(a) Disconnect the electrical connector from the left engine duct overpressure switch.
(b) Connect a jumper wire between pins 1 and 2 of the connector.
1) Make sure that the left COWL ANTI-ICE light comes on.
(c) Remove the jumper wire from the connector.
(d) Re-connect the connector to the overpressure switch.
(e) Make sure that the left COWL ANTI-ICE light is off.
SUBTASK 30-21-00-720-005
(13) Do this test of the right cowl TAI duct overpressure detection circuit:
(a) Disconnect the electrical connector from the right engine duct overpressure switch.
(b) Connect a jumper wire between pins 1 and 2 of the connector.
1) Make sure that the right COWL ANTI-ICE light comes on.
(c) Remove the jumper wire from the connector.
(d) Connect the connector to the overpressure switch.
(e) Make sure that the right COWL ANTI-ICE light is off.
SUBTASK 30-21-00-720-003
(14) Do this test to make sure that the TAI fault is shown:
(a) Do a test of the left cowl TAI system.
1) Put the ENG ANTI-ICE 1 switch in the ON position.
2) Make sure that the amber TAI indication is shown on the CDS display above the left
N1 speed display.
3) Make sure that the left COWL VALVE OPEN light comes on brightly.
4) Put the ENG ANTI-ICE 1 switch in the OFF position.
5) Make sure that the TAI indication on the CDS display goes away.
6) Make sure that the COWL VALVE OPEN light goes off.
(b) Do a test of the right cowl TAI system.
1) Put the ENG ANTI-ICE 2 switch in the ON position.
2) Make sure that the amber TAI indication is shown on the CDS display above the
right N1 speed display.
30-21-00
3) Make sure that the right COWL VALVE OPEN light comes on brightly.
4) Put the ENG ANTI-ICE 2 switch in the OFF position.
5) Make sure that the TAI indication on the CDS display goes away.
6) Make sure that the COWL VALVE OPEN light goes off.

G. Engine Cowl TAI Valve Removal
SUBTASK 30-21-11-020-001
(1) Disconnect the electrical connector DP1303 [10] from the valve [5].
SUBTASK 30-21-11-020-002
(2) Remove the bolt [6], washer [7], and nut [9] that attach the bonding jumper [8] to the valve [5].
(a) Move the bonding jumper [8] away from the valve [5].
SUBTASK 30-21-11-020-007
(3) If it is necessary to remove the bonding jumper [8] from the flange, do these steps:
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT YOU ONLY USE APPROVED SCRAPERS ON THE
AIRPLANE SKIN. SCRAPERS THAT ARE NOT APPROVED CAN MAKE
SCRATCHES ON THE SKIN AND CAUSE FATIGUE CRACKS.
(a) Remove all of the sealant from the connection of the bonding jumper [8] and flange.
1) Use a sealant removal tool, COM-2481, and sealant removal tool handle,
COM-4746.
2) If it is necessary, do these steps to sharpen the scraper.
30-21-11
a) Sharpen the scraper with sandpaper, G50260, or finer by hand or by metal
sharpening wheel (scraper sharpener, COM-4747).
b) If the scraper is sharpened by hand, rub the sharpened scraper on a wiper,
G50262, to remove loose sanding particles.
c) If a metal sharpening wheel is used, rub the sharpened scraper on sandpaper,
G50261, or finer aluminum oxide paper to remove debris and then rub it on
wiper, G50262, to remove loose sanding particles.
(b) Remove the bolt [13], washers [14], and nut [15], that attach the bonding jumper [8] to the
flange.
(c) Remove the bonding jumper [8].
SUBTASK 30-21-11-020-003
(4) Remove the bolts [11] and nuts [12] that attach the aft duct to the fan case bracket.
SUBTASK 30-21-11-020-004
(5) Do these steps to remove the valve [5]:
(a) Disconnect the couplings [1] at each end of the valve [5].
CAUTION
DO NOT APPLY TOO MUCH FORCE TO THE AFT DUCT . DAMAGE TO
THE AFT DUCT CAN OCCUR.
(b) Push the aft duct rearward to disengage the duct flanges.
NOTE: T emporary support can be installed between the fan case bracket and the aft
duct to hold the duct in this position. This will make the removal of the valve
easier.
(c) Remove the valve [5] from the engine.
(d) Remove the seal [2] from the forward end of the valve [5] and aft duct flange.
(e) Install the protective covers on the openings of the ducts and valve [5].

H. Engine Cowl TAI Valve Installation
SUBTASK 30-21-11-420-001
(1) Remove the protective covers from the ducts and valve [5].
SUBTASK 30-21-11-210-001
(2) Examine the seal [2] as follows:
(a) Make sure that the seal [2] do not have cracks, dents, or other damage.
(b) Replace all damaged seal [2].
SUBTASK 30-21-11-420-002
(3) Do these steps to install the valve [5]:
CAUTION
DO NOT APPLY TOO MUCH FORCE TO THE AFT DUCT . DAMAGE TO
THE AFT DUCT CAN OCCUR.
(a) If removed, install a temporary support between the aft duct and fan case bracket to
move the aft duct rearward.
NOTE: T emporary support can be installed between the fan case bracket and aft duct to
hold the duct in this position. This will make the installation of the valve easier.
(b) Install a seal [2] in the forward end of the valve [5] and on the aft duct flange.
(c) Install the valve [5] between the ducts.
1) Make sure that the flow arrow points forward and locking feature on the downstream
flange is correctly engaged.
(d) Remove the temporary support between the aft duct and fan case bracket.
(e) Loosely install the coupling [1] to attach the valve [5] to the ducts.
1) Make sure that the coupling nuts on the couplings are on top and the nuts face
outboard.
CAUTION
MAKE SURE THAT THE COUPLINGS DO NOT APPLY A FORCE ON THE
DUCTS OR THE INLET-COWL TAI VALVE. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY ,
DAMAGE TO THE EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.
(f) Do these steps to tighten the coupling [1]:
1) If it is difficult to attach the valve [5] to the adjacent ducts, remove the lockwire from
the link [4] and adjust the links until no force is applied to the ducts or the valve [5].
2) Tighten the link [4] and install a MS20995C32 lockwire, G01048.
3) Use a torque wrench, STD-1014, to tighten the coupling [1] to 70 in-lb (7.9 N·m) -
85 in-lb (9.6 N·m).
4) Lightly hit the surface of each coupling [1] with a rubber mallet, STD-3906.
5) Use a torque wrench, STD-1014, to tighten the coupling [1] again to 70 in-lb
(7.9 N·m) - 85 in-lb (9.6 N·m).
6) Install the bolts [11] and nuts [12] that attach the aft duct to the fan case bracket.
30-21-11
a) Tighten the bolts [11] to 97.0 in-lb (11.0 N·m) - 103.0 in-lb (11.6 N·m).
<1> Use a torque wrench, STD-1014 to tighten the bolts [11].
SUBTASK 30-21-11-020-005
(4) Connect the electrical connector DP1303 [10] to the valve [5].
(a) Make sure that there is clearance between the TAI valve and adjacent structures.
SUBTASK 30-21-11-760-002
(5) If was removed, install the bonding jumper [8] to the flange.
(a) Clean the bonding surface on the flange with alcohol, B00130.
(b) Attach the bonding jumper [8] to the flange with the bolt [13], washers [14], and nut [15].
(c) Use an non-intrinsically safe bonding meter, COM-614, to measure the resistance
between the bonding jumper [8] and aircraft structure.
RYR 043, 044, 056-063, 067-078, 081, 083-103, 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135, 138-169, 174-179,
182-187, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236, 239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341,
343, 346-377, 386-416; AIRPLANES WITH TAI VALVE P/N 3215618-4 AND ON
1) Make sure that the resistance is 0.001 ohm (1.0 milliohm) or less.
RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 378-385, 417-999; AIRPLANES WITH TAI VALVE P/N 3215618-2/-3
2) Make sure that the resistance is 0.008 ohms (8.0 milliohms) or less.
RYR ALL
(d) Apply the adhesive, A50102, around the connection to encapsulate the bolt [13],
washers [14], and nut [15].
SUBTASK 30-21-11-760-001
(6) Do these steps to connect the bonding jumper [8] to the valve [5]:
(a) Clean the bonding surface on the valve [5] with alcohol, B00130.
(b) Attach the bonding jumper [8] to the valve [5] with the bolt [6], washer [7], and nut [9].
NOTE: To minimize slack between the two attached lugs of the bonding jumper, rotate
the lug at the valve forward and/or the lug on the fan case toward the upward 12
o'clock position of the fan case. Do not allow any riding condition (slack) of the
jumper on the valve or the engine fan case.
(c) Use an non-intrinsically safe bonding meter, COM-614, to measure the resistance
between the bonding jumper [8] and valve [5].
1) Make sure that the resistance is 0.008 ohms (8.0 milliohms) or less.

(3) If the outside air temperature is more than 50°F (10°C), operate the engine cowl anti-icing
system for 30 seconds or less.
(4) There is no time limit for operation of the engine cowl anti-icing system when the temperature
is at or less than 50°F (10°C).
SUBTASK 30-21-11-840-001
(5) Do one of the following steps to provide pneumatic pressure for the ducts:
(a) To use the engines to pressurize the TAI duct, do this task: Start the Engine Procedure
(Selection), TASK 71-00-00-800-807-F00.
(b) To use the Auxiliary Power Unit (APU) to pressurize the TAI duct, do this task: Supply

(c) To use an external air supply to pressurize the TAI duct, do this task: Supply Pressure to

(d) If the APU, external air, or a single engine is used to pressurize the TAI duct, then do
these steps:
1) Put the BLEED 1 and 2 switches on the air conditioning panel in the OFF position.
2) Put the L PACK and R PACK switches on the air conditioning panel in the OFF
position.
3) Make sure that the ISOLATION VALVE switch is in the OPEN or AUTO position.
4) If the APU or external air is used to pressurize the TAI duct, then manually open the
PRSOV valves.
5) If the a single engine is used to pressurize the TAI duct, then manually open the
PRSOV valve on the non-running engine.
6) Open a PRSOV valve, do these steps on the applicable engine or engines:
WARNING
DO THESE SPECIFIED TASKS IN THE CORRECT SEQUENCE
BEFORE YOU OPEN THE THRUST REVERSER: RETRACT THE
LEADING EDGE, DO THE DEACTIVATION PROCEDURES FOR
THE LEADING EDGE AND THE THRUST REVERSER (FOR
GROUND MAINTENANCE), AND OPEN THE FAN COWL
PANELS. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY THE ABOVE SEQUENCE,
INJURIES TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN
OCCUR.
a) Open the left thrust reverser, do this task: Open the Thrust Reverser
(Selection), TASK 78-31-00-010-801-F00.
30-21-11
b) Put the applicable engine BLEED switch, on the P5-10 forward overhead
panel, to the ON position.
WARNING
PRESSURIZE THE BLEED AIR SYSTEM BEFORE YOU OPEN
THE PRSOV. WHEN YOU OPEN THE PRSOV, USE A RACHET
TYPE WRENCH. THE PRSOV CAN OPEN QUICKLY AND THIS
CAN CAUSE THE HANDLE OF THE WRENCH TO TURN. THIS
CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO
EQUIPMENT .
c) Use a wrench on the manual override nut for the PRSOV to put it to the OPEN
position.
d) Make sure that the PRSOV stays in the OPEN position.
NOTE: The PRSOV is spring-loaded to the closed position. If there is
pressure supplied and the PRSOV does not stay open, there may be
a leakage in the PRSOV or in the sense line(s).
WARNING
OBEY THE INSTRUCTIONS IN THE PROCEDURE TO CLOSE
THE THRUST REVERSERS. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY THE
INSTRUCTIONS WHEN YOU CLOSE THE THRUST
REVERSERS, INJURIES TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO
EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.
e) Close the thrust reverser, do this task: Close the Thrust Reverser (Selection),
TASK 78-31-00-010-804-F00.

(6) Do this test of the COWL VALVE OPEN and COWL ANTI-ICE light switches:
(a) Push and release the COWL VALVE OPEN and COWL ANTI-ICE light switches on the
P5-11 panel.
(b) Make sure that each light comes on then goes off.
SUBTASK 30-21-11-860-011
(7) Make sure that the engine and wing anti-ice panel commands the applicable cowl TAI valves
open:
(a) For the left cowl TAI valve, put the ENG ANTI-ICE 1 switch in the ON position.
1) Make sure that the left COWL VALVE OPEN light comes on brightly for 1 to 6
seconds.
2) Make sure that the COWL VALVE OPEN light is on dimly after 6 seconds.
3) Make sure that the green TAI indication shows on the Common Display System
(CDS) display, above the left N1 speed display.
(b) For the right cowl TAI valve, put the ENG ANTI-ICE 2 switch in the ON position.
1) Make sure that the right COWL VALVE OPEN light comes on brightly for 1 to 6
seconds.
2) Make sure that the COWL VALVE OPEN light is on dimly after 6 seconds.
3) Make sure that the green TAI indication shows on the CDS display, above the right
N1 speed display.
30-21-11
SUBTASK 30-21-11-860-015

HOT . IF YOU ARE NOT CAREFUL, INJURIES TO PERSONS CAN OCCUR.
(8) Make sure that there is no leakage around the engine cowl TAI ducts and TAI valve.

SUBTASK 30-21-11-860-012

seconds.
2) Make sure that the COWL VALVE OPEN light goes off after 3 seconds.
(b) For the right cowl TAI valve, put the ENG ANTI-ICE 2 switch in the OFF position.
1) Make sure that the right COWL VALVE OPEN light comes on brightly for 1 to 3
seconds.
2) Make sure that the COWL VALVE OPEN light goes off after 3 seconds.

2. Engine Cowl TAI Valve - Contact Resistance Check
A. General
(1) This procedure provides the steps necessary to perform a contact resistance check of the
electrical contacts for the position switches in the engine cowl Thermal Anti-Ice (TAI) valve.

F . Engine Cowl TAI Valve - Contact Resistance Check
SUBTASK 30-21-11-020-009
(1) Disconnect the electrical connector DP1303 from the valve (TASK 30-21-11-000-801).
SUBTASK 30-21-11-280-001
(2) With the valve in the closed position, measure the resistance between the valve pins as follows
(WDM 30-21-11, WDM 30-21-21):
(a) Make sure that the resistance between Pin 7 and Pin 8 is less than 0.2 ohms.
(b) Make sure that the resistance between Pin 4 and Pin 5 is less than 0.2 ohms.
SUBTASK 30-21-11-280-002
(3) Use a wrench on the manual override to set the valve to the OPEN and locked position.
(a) Measure the resistance between the valve pins as follows (WDM 30-21-11, WDM
30-21-21):
1) Make sure that the resistance between Pin 8 and Pin 9 is less than 0.2 ohms.
2) Make sure that the resistance between Pin 5 and Pin 6 is less than 0.2 ohms.
SUBTASK 30-21-11-860-034
(4) Set the valve to the CLOSED and unlocked position.
SUBTASK 30-21-11-420-005
(5) Connect the electrical connector DP1303 to the valve (TASK 30-21-11-400-801).

30-21-11

(a) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers:

Row Col Number Name
B 6 C00149 ANTI-ICE & RAIN ENGINE 2 CONTROL
B 7 C01002 ANTI-ICE & RAIN ENG 2 COWL AI VALVE
SUBTASK 30-21-11-710-001
(8) During manual operation, if the valve did not move smoothly, do this task: Engine Cowl

2. Honest Orifice - Cleaning
(Figure 701)
A. General
(1) This procedure provides the steps necessary to remove, clean, and install the honest orifice on
those engine cowl Thermal Anti-Ice (TAI) valves that have a removable honest orifice.
B. References
Reference Title
36-00-00-860-806 Remove Pressure from the Pneumatic System (P/B 201)
71-11-02-010-801-F00 Open the Fan Cowl Panels (P/B 201)
71-11-02-410-801-F00 Close the Fan Cowl Panels (P/B 201)
C. Tools/Equipment
Reference Description
STD-182 Cleaner - Ultrasonic
STD-183 Cleaner - Vacuum
STD-6462 Bottle - Squeeze
STD-6638 Air Source - Compressed, Clean, Filtered, and Dry, 0 - 100 psi
D. Consumable Materials
Reference Description Specification
B50270 Cleaner - Bonderite C-AK MXP Aero (Formerly
Turco C MXP)
BAC5749 Method 3
D50179 Compound - Lubricating (Lockrey Liqui-Moly
NV Thread Compound)
G01659 Swab - Cotton Or Rayon, (Disposable)
G50225 Lockwire - MS20995C20, Corrosion Resistant
Steel - 0.020 Inch (0.508 mm) Diameter
NASM20995
E. Location Zones
Zone Area
410 Subzone - Engine 1
420 Subzone - Engine 2
F . Prepare for the Cleaning
SUBTASK 30-21-11-870-001
(1) If not already done, do this task: Remove Pressure from the Pneumatic System,
TASK 36-00-00-860-806.
SUBTASK 30-21-11-860-018
(2) For the right fan cowl panel, do this task: Open the Fan Cowl Panels,
TASK 71-11-02-010-801-F00.
30-21-11
G. Honest Orifice Cleaning
SUBTASK 30-21-11-020-006
(1) Do the following to remove the honest orifice [4]:
(a) Remove the lockwire [1].
(b) Remove the screw [2] and washer [3].
(c) Remove the honest orifice [4].
SUBTASK 30-21-11-170-001
(2) Clean the orifice with one of the following methods:
NOTE: You can replace the honest orifice with a new or serviceable part as an alternative to
cleaning it.
(a) Method one uses compressed air:
1) Apply an air source, STD-6638, through the orifice from the threaded end and at
each of the four vent holes.
2) If necessary, use a 0.032 in. (0.813 mm) lockwire to clean out the internal hole of
the honest orifice.
(b) Method two uses ultrasonic cleaning:
1) Put the orifice in a solution of Bonderite C-AK MXP Aero cleaner, B50270, for 4 to 6
minutes.
2) Flush the orifice internally and externally with clean water at high pressure.
3) Put the removable orifice in a beaker with the open end of the orifice down and fill
the beaker with Bonderite C-AK MXP Aero cleaner, B50270.
4) Put the beaker in an ultrasonic cleaner, STD-182, and fill the tank with water until
the water level is 0.375 in. (9.525 mm) from the top of the beaker.
5) Operate the ultrasonic cleaner for 5 minutes.
6) Remove the orifice from the beaker and discard the dirty cleaning solution.
7) Flush the orifice and beaker with the cleaning solution using a squeeze bottle,
STD-6462.
8) Put the removable orifice back in the beaker with the open end of the orifice down
and fill the beaker with the cleaning solution.
9) Operate the ultrasonic cleaner for another 5 minutes.
10) Repeat the process from step 6 until the cleaning solution is clear after a 5 minute
period of operation in the ultrasonic cleaner.
SUBTASK 30-21-11-100-001
(3) Use swab, G01659, to carefully clean and wipe up dirt from the following:
NOTE: Be careful not to allow dirt to drop down into the hole.
(a) The valve housing.
(b) The honest orifice threads.
SUBTASK 30-21-11-100-002
(4) If unwanted materials fall into the valve port, use the vacuum cleaner, STD-183, to remove
them.
30-21-11
SUBTASK 30-21-11-420-003
CAUTION
OBEY THESE INSTRUCTIONS. IF YOU INSTALL THE HONEST ORIFICE
INCORRECTLY , HOT AIR LEAKS WILL CAUSE DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(5) Use thread lubricating compound for the assembly of all threaded parts as follows:
CAUTION
USE OF TOO MUCH THREAD LUBRICATING COMPOUND CAN CAUSE
CLOGGED VENTS AND ORIFICES.
(a) Apply a small quantity of Liqui-Moly NV Thread Compound, D50179, to the threads of all
male threaded parts.
SUBTASK 30-21-11-420-006
(6) Install the honest orifice [4].
(a) Tighten the honest orifice [4] to 17 in-lb (1.9 N·m) - 20 in-lb (2.3 N·m).
(b) Make sure that there is a minimum of 0.015 in. (0.381 mm) gap under the honest orifice
head for sufficient airflow (View A-A, Figure 701).
SUBTASK 30-21-11-420-007
(7) Install the washer [3] and screw [2].
(a) Tighten the screw [2] to 6 in-lb (0.7 N·m) - 8 in-lb (0.9 N·m).
SUBTASK 30-21-11-160-002
(8) Remove all of the thread lubricating compound that is remaining after assembly of the
threaded parts.
SUBTASK 30-21-11-420-008
(9) Use the lockwire [1] (MS20995C20 lockwire, G50225) to attach both the screw [2] and honest
orifice [4] as one to the housing.
H. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition
SUBTASK 30-21-11-860-020
(1) For the right fan cowl panel, do this task: Close the Fan Cowl Panels,
TASK 71-11-02-410-801-F00.

D. Window Heat System Operational Test
SUBTASK 30-41-00-080-001
CAUTION
ALL PROTECTIVE CAPS AND COVERS MUST BE REMOVED FROM ALL AIR
DATA SENSORS. THE SENSORS AND COVERS COULD BE DAMAGED IF
THERE ARE COVERS INSTALLED ON THE SENSORS WHEN THEY ARE
HEATED.
(1) Make sure that there are no protective covers on the air data sensors.
(a) If protective covers are found, then remove them before you continue this test.
30-41-00
SUBTASK 30-41-00-840-001
(2) Use the Infrared Thermometer, COM-1572, or pyrometer, STD-1179, or other temperature
indicating device near each window temperature sensor to measure the window temperature.
SUBTASK 30-41-00-880-001
(3) If the window temperature is greater than 110°F (43°C), then cool the window.
NOTE: The window can be cooled with cool water or by providing shade from the sun.
SUBTASK 30-41-00-860-001
(4) One switch at a time, put the WINDOW HEAT switches on the Window and Pitot Heat Module
to the on position.
(a) Make sure that the corresponding ON light on the window and pitot heat module comes
on.
NOTE: It can take up to 15 seconds for the light to come on.
(b) Make sure that both master caution lights are off.
SUBTASK 30-41-00-210-001
(5) Wait for the windows to heat up.
(a) Make sure that the front and side windows stabilize at 90°F (32°C) to 120°F (49°C).
NOTE: It can take up to 4 minutes for the window temperatures to stabilize.
RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044, 056-063, 067-071 PRE SB 737-56-1017
(b) Make sure that the number 4 and number 5 windows heat up to more than 90°F (32°C)
but less than 125°F (52°C).
RYR ALL
SUBTASK 30-41-00-860-002
(6) Put the WINDOW HEAT TEST OVHT/PWR switch to the OVHT position for 1 second.
(a) Make sure that the OVERHEAT lights come on.
(b) Make sure that the MASTER CAUTION lights come on.
(c) Make sure that ANTI-ICE light comes on.
(d) Make sure that the ON lights go off in less than 70 seconds.
(e) Make sure that the windows start to cool.
(f) Wait until the window temperatures go below 110°F (43°C).
SUBTASK 30-41-00-860-003
(7) One switch at a time, put each WINDOW HEAT power switch in the off position for 1 second
and then put the switch in the on position.
(a) Make sure that the applicable OVERHEAT light goes off.
(b) Make sure that the window heat ON light comes on.
(c) Make sure that the windows begin to heat.
SUBTASK 30-41-00-860-004
(8) Press either MASTER CAUTION light.
(a) Make sure that the MASTER CAUTION light goes off.
(b) Make sure that the ANTI-ICE light goes off.
SUBTASK 30-41-00-860-005
(9) Wait 4 minutes.
30-41-00
SUBTASK 30-41-00-860-006
(10) Hold the WINDOW HEAT OVHT/PWR TEST switch to PWR TEST not more than 60 seconds.
(a) Make sure that the WINDOW HEAT ON lights stay on for each window.
SUBTASK 30-41-00-860-011
(11) Release the WINDOW HEAT OVHT/PWR TEST switch.
(a) If holding the WINDOW HEAT OVHT/PWR TEST switch to PWR TEST causes the
OVERHEAT light to illuminate, the WINDOW HEAT ON lights shall go off within 70
seconds.
SUBTASK 30-41-00-860-007
(12) Put the WINDOW HEAT power switches in the off position.
SUBTASK 30-41-00-860-010
(13) If the PWR TEST switch caused an overheat condition, then press either MASTER CAUTION
light.
(a) Make sure that the OVERHEAT lights, the MASTER CAUTION lights, and the ANTI-ICE
lights are off.

SUBTASK 30-41-00-900-001
(4) For the No. 2 window, change the wires from the primary sensor to the spare sensor:
(a) Disconnect the coil cord that is attached to the side window.
(b) If it is necessary, remove the side window (TASK 56-12-11-000-801).
(c) Disconnect the wire connected to the primary sensor's D terminal located at the bottom
corner of the window.
(d) Connect the wire to the spare temperature sensor's D terminal.
(e) Apply a layer of Dow Corning 340 heat sink compound, D50244, to the terminals with
swab, G01659.
NOTE: Dow Corning 340 heat sink compound, D50244, decreases the insulation loss
between the sensor terminals and metal frame of the window. If not done, this
can cause the window to become too hot.
(f) If removed, install the side window (TASK 56-12-11-400-801).
(g) Connect the coil cord to the side window.
RYR ALL POST SB 737-30-1078; AIRPLANES WITH QUICK DISCONNECT PLUGS
SUBTASK 30-41-00-900-002
(5) For the No. 2 left (right) window, change the wires from the primary sensor to the spare sensor:
(a) Disconnect the coil cord that is attached to the side window.
(b) Remove the quick disconnect plugs from the channel.
(c) Disconnect the quick disconnect plug D7701P (D7702P) from the primary sensor quick
disconnect receptacle D7701J (D7702J).
(d) Remove the cap from the spare sensor quick disconnect plug D7703 (D7704).
(e) Connect the spare sensor quick disconnect plug D7703 (D7704) to the quick disconnect
plug D7701P (D7702P).
(f) Install a cap on the primary sensor quick disconnect receptacle D7701J (D7702J).
(g) Stow the quick disconnect plugs in the channel.
(h) Connect the coil cord to the side window.

30-41-11-440-801Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU) Override Mode Activation/DeactivationPDF p. 241
Open original AMM PDF - page 241 ↗ 
2. Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU) Override Mode Activation/Deactivation
A. General
(1) This task includes the steps to activate/deactivate the OVERRIDE mode on the Window Heat
Control Unit (WHCU).
(2) The WHCU 1 is on the E2-1 rack and supplies power for the No. 1 left and No. 2 right
windows.
(3) The WHCU 2 is on the E4-2 rack and supplies power for the No. 1 right and No. 2 left
windows.
(4) In the OVERRIDE mode, the OVERHEAT lights will come on when the two window heat
sensors become defective.
B. Location Zones
Zone Area
117 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Left
118 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Right
211 Flight Compartment - Left
212 Flight Compartment - Right
C. Access Panels
Number Name/Location
117A Electronic Equipment Access Door

SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-036
(1) Make sure that these circuit breakers are closed:
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-11
Row Col Number Name
B 8 C00393 WINDOW HEAT POWER RIGHT SIDE
B 9 C00228 WINDOW HEAT POWER LEFT FRONT
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-12
Row Col Number Name
B 8 C00394 WINDOW HEAT POWER RIGHT FRONT
B 9 C00392 WINDOW HEAT POWER LEFT SIDE
SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-025
(2) To get access to the electronic equipment center, do this step:
30-41-11
(a) Open this access panel:
Number Name/Location
117A Electronic Equipment Access Door
E. OVERRIDE Mode Activation/Deactivation for No. 1 Window
SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-026
(1) Activate/deactivate the OVERRIDE mode for the No. 1 window:
(a) Set the applicable WINDOW HEAT switch, on the P5-9 panel, to the OFF position.
(b) Set the applicable WINDOW HEAT switch, on the P5-9 panel, to the ON position.
(c) For the No. 1 left window:
1) Push and hold the LAMP TEST and BIT VERIFY buttons on the WHCU 1 for 5
seconds.
(d) For the No. 1 right window:
1) Push and hold the LAMP TEST and BIT VERIFY buttons on the WHCU 2 for 5
seconds.
(e) For activation, make sure that the #1 WINDOW green OVERRIDE MODE light comes on.
(f) For deactivation, make sure that the #1 WINDOW green OVERRIDE MODE light goes
off.
(g) Set the applicable WINDOW HEAT switch, on the P5-9 panel, to the OFF position.
RYR 397-999; Airplanes with the dual sensor operation (WHCU program pin P1-5 is grounded)
F . OVERRIDE Mode Activation/Deactivation for No. 2 Window
NOTE: The No. 2 window dual sensor configuration and OVERRIDE mode is available only for
airplanes with the two WHCU configuration with part number 83000-2790X. To use dual
sensor configuration on the No. 2 window, the WHCU program pin (P1-5) should be
grounded and the two window sensors should be connected.
SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-031
(1) Activate/deactivate the OVERRIDE mode for the No. 2 window:
(a) Set the applicable WINDOW HEAT switch, on the P5-9 panel, to the OFF position.
(b) Set the applicable WINDOW HEAT switch, on the P5-9 panel, to the ON position.
(c) For the No. 2 left window:
1) Push and hold the FAULT HISTORY and BIT LAMP RESET buttons on the WHCU 2
for 5 seconds.
(d) For the No. 2 right window:
1) Push and hold the FAULT HISTORY and BIT LAMP RESET buttons on the WHCU 1
for 5 seconds.
(e) For activation, make sure that the #2 WINDOW green OVERRIDE MODE light comes on.
(f) For deactivation, make sure that the #2 WINDOW green OVERRIDE MODE light goes
off.
(g) Set the applicable WINDOW HEAT switch, on the P5-9 panel, to the OFF position.
RYR 397-999
30-41-11

SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-028

H. Heat the Window - Heat Gun
SUBTASK 30-41-21-880-002
(1) Do these steps to heat the windows if the temperature is too cold:
NOTE: These steps can be used if the ambient temperature is significantly less the specified
test temperature range of 68°F (20°C) - 108° (42°C).
(a) Heat the window near the sensor with a 180 deg. F (82 deg. C) maximum output
temperature heat gun, STD-442, for up to thirty seconds.
NOTE: You can use an Infrared Thermometer, COM-1572 (including pyrometer,
STD-1179), or other temperature indicating device to do a check of the
temperature.
SUBTASK 30-41-21-010-010
(2) Remove the applicable WHCU for the window.
(a) Remove the WHCU, do this task: Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU) Removal,
TASK 30-41-11-000-803.
30-41-21
RYR 397-999 (Continued)
I. Resistance of the Window Temperature Sensors Measurement
SUBTASK 30-41-21-760-005
(1) Measure the resistance of the applicable window temperature sensor.
(a) Apply pressure to the corner near the temperature sensor terminal when measuring the
resistance of the sensors.
NOTE: It is necessary to apply the pressure to the terminals to find an intermittent
connection that can occur at any point along the temperature sensor.
(b) Make sure that the resistance of the sensor is within the limits shown on Figure 505.
Table 503/30-41-21-993-805
WINDOW TEMP SENSOR CONNECTOR/PINS WHCU WDM
LEFT FRONT 1 (Primary) D16230/ 19 TO 25 M321 30-41-11
LEFT FRONT 1 (Spare) D16230/ 26 TO 27 M321 30-41-11
RIGHT SIDE 2 (Primary) D16230/ 17 TO 18 M321 30-41-11
RIGHT SIDE 2 (Spare) D16230/ 17 TO 18 M321 30–41–11
RIGHT FRONT 1 (Primary) D16232/ 19 TO 25 M323 30-41-12
RIGHT FRONT 1 (Spare) D16232/ 26 TO 27 M323 30-41-12
LEFT SIDE 2 (Primary) D16232/ 17 TO 18 M323 30-41-12
LEFT SIDE 2 (Spare) D16232/ 17 TO 18 M323 30–41–12
NOTE: Window temperatures of 68°F (20°C) - 108°F (42°C) are expected for this test.
NOTE: If the temperature of any individual window in Table 503 is greater than 108°F
(42°C) due to environmental condition(s), then a measured sensor resistance
value less than 362 ohms is acceptable.
NOTE: A measured sensor resistance value above 362 ohms indicates a failed sensor.
SUBTASK 30-41-21-760-006
(2) Do this test to measure the resistance of the spare sensors for the side windows (Table 503):
NOTE: This test applies to spare sensors without quick disconnect plugs.
(a) Move the rubber terminal boot covers to expose electrical terminals and mounting
screws.
NOTE: The terminals are found at the bottom aft corner of the No. 2 Windows.
(b) Examine the terminals for corrosion.
1) If it is necessary, clean the terminals.
(c) Remove the D terminal lug that is attached to the primary sensor.
(d) Install the D terminal lug to the spare sensor.
(e) Make sure that the resistance is within the limits (Figure 505).
(f) Remove the D terminal lug that is attached to the spare sensor.
(g) Install the D terminal lug to the primary sensor.
30-41-21
RYR 397-999 (Continued)
(h) Apply a layer of Dow Corning 340 heat sink compound, D50244, to the terminals with the
swab, G01659.
NOTE: The Dow Corning 340 heat sink compound, D50244, is intended to prevent
inadvertent loss of insulation between the sensor terminals and the metal frame
of the window, that can cause the window to overheat.
(i) Install the rubber terminal boot covers over the terminals as required.
1) Make sure that the rubber terminal boot covers are securely installed to avoid
dislocation.
SUBTASK 30-41-21-760-008
(3) Do this test to measure the resistance of the spare sensors for the side windows (Table 503):
NOTE: This test applies to spare sensors with quick disconnect plugs.
(a) Remove the quick disconnect plugs from the channel.
(b) Disconnect the quick disconnect plug for the primary sensor.
(c) If it is necessary, examine the quick disconnect plugs for the primary sensor and spare
sensor for corrosion and clean.
(d) Connect the quick disconnect plug for the spare sensor.
(e) Make sure that the resistance is within the limits (Figure 505).
(f) Disconnect the quick disconnect plug for the spare sensor and reconnect the quick
disconnect plug to the primary sensor.
(g) Place the quick disconnect plugs back in the channel.
J. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition
SUBTASK 30-41-21-420-003
(1) Install the WHCU (TASK 30-41-11-400-803).

SUBTASK 30-41-11-710-002
(2) Do the WHCU [1] Built-In-Test Equipment (BITE) test:
(a) Put the WINDOW HEAT switches, on the P5–9 forward overhead panel, to the ON
position.
(b) Press the LAMP TEST switch.
1) Make sure that all lights (10 red, 4 green) come on.
2) If any light is off, replace the WHCU [1].
(c) Press the BIT VERIFY switch for 1 second.
1) Make sure that no red lights remain on and that the green BIT TEST OK light comes
on in less than 10 seconds and stays on for 15 seconds.
2) If any red light stays on, then a fault exists. Refer to the FIM and do the applicable
procedure(s).
(d) Push the FAULT HISTORY switch.
1) Make sure that the last fault is shown.
(e) Push the BIT LAMP RESET switch.
1) Make sure that all lights are off.
(f) Push the LAMP TEST and BIT LAMP RESET switches simultaneously and hold for 4
seconds.
1) Make sure that the NVRAM is erased.
(g) Push the LAMP TEST and BIT VERIFY switches simultaneously and hold for 4 seconds.
1) Make sure that the module is in the #1 WINDOW OVERRIDE mode.
(h) If necessary, push and hold the LAMP TEST switch and BIT VERIFY switch again to turn
off the #1 WINDOW OVERRIDE mode.
(i) Put the WINDOW HEAT switches, on the P5–9 forward overhead panel, to the OFF
position.

2. Window Heat Control Unit System - Test
(Figure 501)
A. General
(1) The Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU)s are installed in the electrical/electronic (EE)
compartment. A Built-In-Test Equipment (BITE) module is installed on all the control units. The
test makes sure the WHCUs operate properly.
(2) The WHCUs can be tested separately or they can all be tested at the same time.
B. Location Zones
Zone Area
117 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Left
118 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Right
211 Flight Compartment - Left
212 Flight Compartment - Right
C. Access Panels
Number Name/Location
117A Electronic Equipment Access Door
