SUBTASK 30-41-11-010-004
(1) Open this access panel to get access to the EE compartment:
Number Name/Location
117A Electronic Equipment Access Door
SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-002
(2) Press and release the BIT LAMP RESET switch on the WHCUs.
(a) Make sure all the indicator lights are off.
E. Test the Normal Functions of the WHCUs
SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-003
(1) Make sure the window temperature is less than 110°F (43°C).
NOTE: If the window temperature is more than 110°F (43°C), WHCU will not supply electrical
power to heat the window.
(2) One switch at a time, put the WINDOW HEAT power switches on the window/pitot heat control
panel, P5–9, to the ON position.
NOTE: If you are only testing one WHCU, you only need to put the applicable switch in the
ON position. The table in (Figure 501) shows which WHCU controls each window heat.
30-41-11
(a) Make sure that the corresponding ON light on the window/pitot heat control panel comes
on.
NOTE: It can take up to 15 seconds for a light to come on.
NOTE: If the window temperature is more than 110°F (43°C) corresponding ON light on
the window/pitot heat control panel stays off.
(b) Make sure that both MASTER CAUTION lights on the glareshield panel, P7, are off.
SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-004
(3) Put the WINDOW HEAT OVHT/PWR TEST switch to the OVHT position for 1 second.
(a) Make sure that the OVERHEAT lights come on.
(b) Make sure the MASTER CAUTION lights come on.
(c) Make sure ANTI-ICE light comes on.
(d) Make sure the ON lights go off in less than 70 seconds.
SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-005
(4) One switch at a time, put each window heat control switch in the OFF position for 1 second
and then put the switch in the ON position.
(a) Make sure that the applicable OVERHEAT light goes off.
(b) Make sure the window heat ON light comes on.
SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-006
(5) Press either MASTER CAUTION light.
(a) Make sure that the MASTER CAUTION light goes off.
(b) Make sure that the ANTI-ICE light goes off.
SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-007
(6) Wait 4 minutes.
SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-008
(7) Hold the WINDOW HEAT OVHT/ PWR TEST switch to PWR TEST not more than 60 seconds.
(a) Make sure that the WINDOW HEAT ON lights stay on for each window.
SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-019
(8) Release the WINDOW HEAT OVHT/ PWR TEST switch.
(a) If holding the WINDOW HEAT OVHT/ PWR TEST switch to PWR TEST causes the
OVERHEAT light to illuminate, the window heat ON lights shall go off within 70 seconds.
SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-009
(9) Put the WINDOW HEAT power switches in the OFF position.

(a) Make sure that the green BIT TEST OK lights come on for 15 seconds.

306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-396
(a) Make sure that all six lights come on.
RYR 397-999
(b) Make sure that all fourteen lights come on.
RYR ALL

30-41-21-000-801Check the Electrical Resistance of the Window Heat FilmPDF p. 267

2. Check the Electrical Resistance of the Window Heat Film
(Figure 501)
A. General
(1) The number 1 and number 2 windows have a layer of material which heat the windows when
electricity is applied. The Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU) provides power to heat the
windows. The WHCUs have various output connections which should be matched to each
window resistance. This test checks the electrical resistance of the window heaters and makes
sure the heaters are matched to the WHCU.
B. References
Reference Title
30-41-11-000-801 Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU) Removal (P/B 401)
30-41-11-400-801 Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU) Installation (P/B 401)
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
117 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Left
118 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Right
121 Forward Cargo Compartment - Left
122 Forward Cargo Compartment - Right
D. Prepare for the Procedure
SUBTASK 30-41-21-010-001
(1) Remove the applicable WHCU for the window (TASK 30-41-11-000-801).
NOTE: See Table 501 to identify which WHCU needs to be removed.
SUBTASK 30-41-21-010-002
(2) Remove the panels on the forward end of the forward cargo compartment to access the
window heat connections on the back of the shelves (Figure 501).
E. Check of the Electrical Resistance of the Window Heat Film
SUBTASK 30-41-21-020-001
(1) Remove the applicable connector from the applicable shelf (Figure 501 and Table 501).
SUBTASK 30-41-21-760-001
(2) Measure the resistance from the applicable connector pin to airplane ground (Table 501).

(1) Make sure that the window temperature is between 68°F (20°C) and 108°F (42°C).

30-41-21

(b) If the window temperature is between 68°F (20°C) and 108°F (42°C), remove the

Removal, TASK 30-41-11-000-801.
G. Heat the Window - WHCU
SUBTASK 30-41-21-880-004
(1) Do these steps to heat the windows if the temperature is too cold:
NOTE: These steps can be used if the ambient temperature is significantly less the specified
test temperature range of 68°F (20°C) to 108°F (42°C).
(a) One switch at a time, put the WINDOW HEAT switches, on the window and pitot heat
module, to the ON position.
1) Make sure that the corresponding ON light on the window and pitot heat module
comes on.
NOTE: It can take up to 15 seconds for the light to come on.
2) Make sure that both master caution lights are off.
(b) Wait for the windows to heat up.
1) Use the Infrared Thermometer, COM-1572, or pyrometer, STD-1179, or other
temperature indicating device near each window temperature sensor to measure
the window temperature.
2) Make sure that the front and side windows stabilize at 90°F (32°C) to 120°F (49°C).
NOTE: It can take up to 4 minutes for the window temperatures to stabilize.
(c) Put the WINDOW HEAT power switches in the OFF position.
1) Make sure that the OVERHEAT lights, MASTER CAUTION lights, and ANTI-ICE
lights are off.
SUBTASK 30-41-21-010-008
(2) Remove the applicable WHCU for the window (Table 502).
(a) Do this task: Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU) Removal, TASK 30-41-11-000-801.
H. Heat the Window - Heat Gun
SUBTASK 30-41-21-880-005
(1) Do these steps to heat the windows if the temperature is too cold:
NOTE: These steps can be used if the ambient temperature is significantly less the specified
test temperature range of 68°F (20°C) to 108°F (42°C).
(a) Heat the window near the sensor with a 180 deg. F (82 deg. C) maximum output
temperature heat gun, STD-442, for up to thirty seconds.
NOTE: You can use the Infrared Thermometer, COM-1572, or pyrometer, STD-1179, or
other temperature indicating device to do a check of the temperature.
SUBTASK 30-41-21-010-007
(2) Remove the applicable WHCU for the window (Table 502).
(a) Do this task: Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU) Removal, TASK 30-41-11-000-801.

I. Window Temperature Sensors Resistance Measurement
SUBTASK 30-41-21-760-002
(1) Measure the resistance of the applicable window temperature sensor.
(a) Apply pressure to the corner near the temperature sensor terminal when measuring the
resistance of the sensors.
NOTE: It is necessary to apply the pressure to the terminals to find an intermittent
connection that can occur at any point along the temperature sensor.
(b) Make sure that the resistance of the sensor is within the limits (Figure 503).
Table 502/30-41-21-993-804

LEFT SIDE 2 D1046A/ 13 TO 26 M322 30-41-12

NOTE: Window temperatures of 68°F (20°C) - 108°F (42°C) are expected for this test.
NOTE: If the temperature of any individual window in Table 502 is greater than 108°F
(42°C) due to environmental condition(s), then a measured sensor resistance
value less than 362 ohms is acceptable.
NOTE: A measured sensor resistance value above 362 ohms indicates a failed sensor.

screws.

(b) Examine the terminals for corrosion.
1) If it is necessary, clean the terminals.
(c) Remove the D terminal lug that is attached to the primary sensor.
30-41-21
RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044, 056-063, 067-078, 081, 083-103, 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135,

(f) Remove the D terminal lug that is attached to the spare sensor.
(g) Install the D terminal lug to the primary sensor.
(h) Apply a layer of Dow Corning 340 heat sink compound, D50244, to the terminals with a
swab, G01659.
NOTE: The Dow Corning 340 heat sink compound, D50244, is intended to prevent
inadvertent loss of insulation between the sensor terminals and the metal frame
of the window, that can cause the window to overheat.
(i) Install the rubber terminal boot covers over the terminals as required.
1) Make sure that the rubber terminal boot covers are securely installed to avoid
dislocation.

4. Check the Electrical Resistance of the Window Heat Film
A. General
(1) The number 1 and number 2 windows have a layer of material which heat the windows when
electricity is applied. The window heat control units (WHCUs) provide power to heat the
windows. The WHCUs will measure the resistance of the window at start-up and then adjust
the output voltage and power accordingly.
B. References
Reference Title

(1) Remove the applicable WHCU for the window, do this task: Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU)
Removal, TASK 30-41-11-000-803.
E. Electrical Resistance of the Window Heat Film Check
SUBTASK 30-41-21-970-002
(1) Measure the resistance at each connector to ground as follows.
(a) Unit 1 Left Fwd M321 D16230 pin 1 to ground.
(b) Unit 1 Right side M321 D16230 pin 3 to ground.
(c) Unit 2 Right Fwd M323 D16232 pin 1 to ground.
(d) Unit 2 Left side M323 D16232 pin 3 to ground.
SUBTASK 30-41-21-970-003
(2) Make sure that the window resistance is within the range of 31.4 to 52 ohms for the front
windows (No. 1), and 55.7 to 100 ohms for the side windows (No. 2).

6. Measure the Insulation Resistance and Dielectric Absorption Ratio of the Control Cabin Windows
(Figure 506)
A. General
(1) This Predictive Maintenance & Enhanced Troubleshooting (PMET) procedure is optional and
the results are for evaluation purposes only. No action is required as a result of this inspection
if a flight deck effect or other observed fault does not exist. The recommendations are made to
show Boeing's best practices to make sure that maximum aircraft reliability is reached. If a
flight deck effect or other observed fault exists, it must be corrected with accepted procedures.
(2) This test measures the Insulation Resistance (IR) and Dielectric Absorption Rate (DAR) of the
control cabin window heat sensors, conductive coating, and bus bars. A low dielectric
insulation resistance value between the components in the window can cause the system to
not operate correctly. A check of these values can help to monitor the health of the components
in the window.
(3) This test only does a check of the sensor on the L2/R2 window that is connected to the
system. To do a check of the spare sensor on the L2/R2 window, refer to WDM 30-41-11 or
WDM 30-41-12 to change the connection of the sensor to the circuit.
B. References
Reference Title
20-10-39-700-801 Insulation Resistance Testing - Special Detail Inspection
(P/B 501)
24-22-00-860-811 Supply Electrical Power (P/B 201)
24-22-00-860-812 Remove Electrical Power (P/B 201)
25-11-21-000-801 Flight Compartment Forward Ceiling Panel Removal (P/B 201)
30-41-11-000-803 Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU) Removal (P/B 401)
30-41-11-400-803 Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU) Installation (P/B 401)
30-41-11-710-801 Window Heat Control Unit System - Test (P/B 501)
WDM 30-41-11 Wiring Diagram Manual
WDM 30-41-12 Wiring Diagram Manual

G. Window Heat Insulation Resistance Check
SUBTASK 30-41-21-710-003
(1) Use the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or equivalent meter to do the IR and
DAR test of the control cabin L2 and R2 windows (TASK 20-10-39-700-801):
NOTE: The Fluke multimeter will show an “ERR” message at the end of the test if the DAR
value is more than 1.6.
NOTE: The Megger multimeter will show 3 horizontal lines on the display at the end of the test
if the DAR value is more than 1.6.
(a) Do an IR check of the circuit insulation between the conductive coating and the sensor,
and between the sensor and the Ground Bus Bar on the L2 and R2 windows:
1) Connect the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or equivalent meter to
the first test point on Table 504.
2) Set the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798 or equivalent meter to 50 Volts
Direct Current (VDC).
NOTE: This check makes sure that the circuits are isolated and there are no shorts.
3) Push the TEST button on the meter for a minimum of 15 seconds.
a) If the value shown on the meter is above 50 megohms, continue.
b) If the value is below 50 megohms, do a check of the wiring between the
applicable M2675 coil cord assembly and the window:
<1> If the wiring is satisfactory, replace the applicable window.
4) Do the check again at each point shown in Table 504.
a) Do not record the values found.
(b) Do an IR check of the circuit insulation between the conductive coating and the sensor,
and between the sensor and the Ground Bus Bar on the L2 and R2 windows:
1) Connect the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or equivalent meter to
the first test point on Table 504.
2) Set the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or equivalent meter to 500
VDC.
3) Push the TEST button on the meter for a minimum 10 seconds.
4) Record the IR value found in Table 504.
5) Do the check again at each point shown in Table 504.
(c) Do a DAR check between the conductive coating and the sensor, and between the
sensor and the Ground Bus Bar on the L2 and R2 windows:
1) Connect the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or equivalent meter to
the first test point on Table 504.
2) Select the DAR function on the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or
equivalent meter.
3) Set the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or equivalent meter to 500
VDC.
4) Push the TEST button on the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or
equivalent meter.
30-41-21
RYR 397-999 (Continued)
5) Record the DAR value found in Table 504.
6) Do the check again at each point shown in Table 504.
Table 504/30-41-21-993-812 L2/R2 Window IR and DAR Values
Test Point Control Unit Connector Measurement Negative Lead Positive Lead IR Value DAR Value
1 Left Side M2675 Sensor to Film 1 4
2 Left Side M2675 Sensor to Ground Ground 1

4 Right Side M2675 Sensor to Film 1 4
5 Right Side M2675 Sensor to Ground Ground 1
6 Right Side M2675 Film to Ground Ground 4
SUBTASK 30-41-21-710-002
(2) Use the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or equivalent meter to do the IR and
DAR test of the control cabin L1 and R1 windows (Insulation Resistance Testing - Special
Detail Inspection, TASK 20-10-39-700-801):
NOTE: The Fluke multimeter will show an “ERR” message at the end of the test if the DAR
value is more than 1.6.
NOTE: The Megger multimeter will show 3 horizontal lines on the display at the end of the test
if the DAR value is more than 1.6.
(a) Do an IR check of the circuit insulation between the conductive coating and the sensor,
and between the sensor and the Ground Bus Bar on the L1 and R1 windows:
1) Connect the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or equivalent meter to
the first test point on Table 505.
2) Set the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or equivalent meter to 50
VDC.
NOTE: This check makes sure that the circuits are isolated and there are no shorts.
3) Push the TEST button on the meter for a minimum of 15 seconds.
a) If the value shown on the meter is above 50 megohms, continue.
b) If the value is below 50 megohms, do a check of the wiring between coil cord
(D10562) [8] (coil cord (D10562) [9]) and the applicable window:
<1> If the wiring is satisfactory, replace the applicable window.
4) Do the check again at each point shown in Table 505.
a) Do not record the values found.
(b) Do an IR check of the circuit insulation between the conductive coating and the sensor
tap, and between the Sensor and the Ground Bus Bar on the L1 and R1 windows:
1) Connect the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or equivalent meter to
the first test point on Table 505.
2) Set the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or equivalent meter to 500
VDC.
3) Push the TEST button on the meter for a minimum 10 seconds.
4) Record the IR value found in Table 505.

5) Do the check again at each point shown in Table 505.

Sensor and the Ground Bus Bar on the L1 and R1 windows:
1) Connect the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or equivalent meter to

2) Select the DAR function on the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or
equivalent meter.
3) Set the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or equivalent meter to 500

equivalent meter.

30-41-21-700-802Measure the Insulation Resistance and Dielectric Absorption Ratio of the Control Cabin WindowsPDF p. 297
Open original AMM PDF - page 297 ↗ 
7. Measure the Insulation Resistance and Dielectric Absorption Ratio of the Control Cabin Windows
(Figure 507)
A. General
(1) This test measures the Insulation Resistance (IR) and Dielectric Absorption Rate (DAR) of the
control cabin window heat sensors, conductive coating, and bus bars. A low dielectric
insulation resistance value between the components in the window can cause the system to
not operate correctly. A check of these values can help to monitor the health of the components
in the window.
(2) This test only does a check of the sensor on the L2/R2 window that is connected to the
system. To do a check of the spare sensor on the L2/R2 window, refer to WDM 30-41-11 or
WDM 30-41-12 to change the connection of the sensor to the circuit.

Reference Title

30-41-31-000-801Thermal Switch RemovalPDF p. 313
Open original AMM PDF - page 313 ↗ 
2. Thermal Switch Removal
(Figure 401)
A. General
(1) This task gives instructions to remove the Thermal Switch.
(2) The window number 5 thermal switches are installed near the top of the window.
(3) The windows have markings to indicate where to install the switch. A spring holds the thermal
switch against the window.
B. Location Zones
Zone Area
212 Flight Compartment - Right
C. Prepare for the Removal
SUBTASK 30-41-31-860-001
(1) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3

D. Thermal Switch Removal
SUBTASK 30-41-31-020-001
(1) Remove the thermal switch [2].
(a) Disconnect the electrical leads [1] from the thermal switch [2].
(b) Pull the spring from the thermal switch [2].
(c) Remove the thermal switch [2] from the window.
NOTE: Be careful not to damage the fairing compound. If the switch does not come off
by hand, you may tap the switch side with a no bounce hammer. If you use a
hammer to remove the switch, you will have to discard the switch.

3. Thermal Switch Installation
(Figure 401)
A. Tools/Equipment
NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the
tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers
that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional.
Reference Description
COM-1572 Thermometer - Infrared, Intrinsically Safe
Part #: 568 EX Supplier: 89536
Part #: 568 EX/BR Supplier: 89536
Part #: 568 EX/CN Supplier: 89536
Part #: 568 EX/ETL Supplier: 89536
Opt Part #: DHS24XC-FM Supplier: 08086
Opt Part #: DHS24XF-FM Supplier: 08086
Opt Part #: EX-MP4 A Supplier: 3GT36
Opt Part #: IR-16L3 IS Supplier: 75037
STD-1179 Tester - Pyrometer
B. Consumable Materials
Reference Description Specification
A00214 Compound - Honeycomb Edge Filling &
Potting, Epoxy Based, 2 Part RT Cure (20 Min
Gel)
BMS5-28 Type 3
A50061 Adhesive - Epoxy BAC5010 Type 54
B00083 Solvent - VM&P Naphthas TT-N-95 Type II, ASTM
D-3735 Type III
D00095 Lubricant - Dry Film - MS122
G50427 Compound - Thermal Heat Sink MIL-DTL-47113
C. Location Zones
Zone Area
212 Flight Compartment - Right
D. Prepare to Install the Thermal Switch
SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-008
(1) Remove the old fairing compound from the window.
SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-009
(2) Clean the face of the thermal switch [2] with solvent, B00083.
SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-010
(3) Clean the surface of the window where it will touch the thermal switch [2] with solvent, B00083.
SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-011
(4) Prepare the fairing compound, A00214, or thermal heat sink compound, G50427, that bonds
the thermal switch [2] to the window.
NOTE: Optional fairing compounds include: Granger 610-1016 DC340 or adhesive, A50061.
SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-012
(5) Find the correct location to install the thermal switch [2].
NOTE: It could be necessary to change the position of the bracket.
30-41-31
(a) Look for the arrow or vertical line that is machined onto the metal insert, visible from the
inboard surface of the window.
1) If the window does not have the arrow or vertical line, locate the label that reads
"LOCATE SWITCH".
(b) Put the vertical center-line of the thermal switch [2] as near as possible to the location
indicated by the positioning arrow or vertical line.
1) If there is no arrow or vertical line, place center-line of thermal switch within 1 in.
(25.4 mm) of label.
SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-013
NOTE: Omit this step when using thermal heat sink compound, G50427.
(6) Put MS122 lubricant, D00095, on the surface of the thermal switch [2] that touches the
window.
SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-014
(7) Put the excessive thermal heat sink compound, G50427, or compound, A00214, on the face of
the thermal switch [2] that touches the window.
NOTE: Optional fairing compounds include GRANGER 610-1016 DC340 and adhesive,
A50061.
CAUTION
DO NOT APPLY TOO MUCH COMPOUND ON THE SWITCH. TOO MUCH
COMPOUND CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE WINDOW.
(a) Use only a very thin layer of thermal heat sink compound, G50427, or compound,
A00214, just enough to cover the surface of the switch when it is against the window with
the retaining spring engaged.
E. Thermal Switch Installation
SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-003
(1) Put the thermal switch [2] in the spring bracket.
(a) Pull the spring back.
(b) Make sure the terminals on the thermal switch [2] are at the hinge of the spring.
(c) Engage the spring with the slot on the rear of the thermal switch [2].
(d) Let the spring push the thermal switch [2] against the window.
(e) Release the spring.
(f) Remove all thermal heat sink compound, G50427, or compound, A00214, that comes out
against the window.
SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-015
NOTE: Omit this step and its substeps when using thermal heat sink compound, G50427.
(2) Make sure the bond is satisfactory.
NOTE: Permit one hour for the sealant to become hard. When the temperature is less than
50°F (10°C), more time is necessary.
(a) Look through the window at the bond.
(b) Make a new bond if you see the following:
1) Bubbles with a diameter of more than 3/32 in. (2.4 mm).
2) Bubbles that add to more than 10 percent of the bond.
30-41-31
SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-016
(3) Clean excess thermal heat sink compound, G50427, or compound, A00214, from the area of
the bond and the thermal switch [2] with solvent, B00083.
SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-018
(4) If it is necessary, trim the liner and re-install the trim around the window.
NOTE: It is acceptable to leave the notch from the previous switch as is.
SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-004
(5) Connect the electrical leads [1] to the thermal switch [2].
F . Thermal Switch Installation Test
SUBTASK 30-41-31-710-001
(1) Do a test of the thermal switch [2].
(a) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
D 1 C00226 WINDOW HEAT CONTROL RIGHT FRONT AC
D 2 C00225 WINDOW HEAT CONTROL LEFT SIDE AC
E 1 C00224 WINDOW HEAT CONTROL LEFT FRONT AC

F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-11
Row Col Number Name
RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044 PRE SB 737-56-1017
B 7 C00229 WINDOW HEAT POWER L4 & L5
RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044, 056-063, 067-071 PRE SB 737-56-1017
B 8 C00393 WINDOW HEAT POWER RIGHT SIDE
B 9 C00228 WINDOW HEAT POWER LEFT FRONT
F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-12
Row Col Number Name
RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044 PRE SB 737-56-1017
B 7 C00395 WINDOW HEAT POWER R4 & R5
RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044, 056-063, 067-071 PRE SB 737-56-1017
B 8 C00394 WINDOW HEAT POWER RIGHT FRONT
B 9 C00392 WINDOW HEAT POWER LEFT SIDE
(b) Push the applicable window heat switch to the ON position.
(c) Monitor the inner temperature of the window adjacent to the thermal switch [2] with
pyrometer, STD-1179, or Infrared Thermometer, COM-1572.
(d) If the temperature stays between 65°F (18.3°C) and 105°F (40.6°C) for window No. 3 and
80°F (27°C) and 125°F (52°C) for window No. 5, then you have corrected the fault.
NOTE: It may take more than 15 minutes for the window to heat.
SUBTASK 30-41-31-860-002
(2) Put the window heat switch in the OFF position.

SUBTASK 30-42-00-010-001
(2) Use a work platform, COM-1523, to get access to the windshield wiper.
SUBTASK 30-42-00-710-001
(3) Do these steps to make sure that the windshield wiper system operates correctly:
CAUTION
DO NOT OPERATE THE WIPER ON A DRY OR DIRTY WINDSHIELD.
ALWAYS SUPPLY WATER TO THE WINDSHIELD BEFORE YOU
OPERATE THE WIPER. THE WIPER WILL CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE
WINDSHIELD.
(a) Supply a continuous spray of clean water to the windshield.
NOTE: For windshields with a hydrophobic coating, deionized or distilled water is
recommended during the wiper test. Water with a high mineral content can cause
marks on the coated glass surface that are difficult to remove. If water with a high
mineral content is used on a windshield with a hydrophobic coating, flush the
windshield surface after the wiper test with deionized or distilled water, or remove
the remaining water on the windshield with a cotton cloth.
(b) Do these steps to test the intermittent speed operation of the wiper:
1) Set the windshield wiper switch to INT (P5 overhead panel flight compartment).
NOTE: The test for the pilot's and first officer's wipers are the same. Use the
applicable wiper switch to operate the wiper.
2) Make sure that the wiper blade parks near the lower edge of the window during the
off time of the intermittent cycle.
3) Make sure that the wiper blades do not touch the metal structure.
NOTE: Minimum overlap between the wiper blades and the windshield seal is
permitted.
4) Make sure that the operation of the wiper is smooth and it clears the water from the
windshield.
30-42-00
5) Make sure that the wipers make two complete strokes every 6 to 8 seconds.
NOTE: One stroke = one wipe.
(c) Do these steps to test the low speed operation of the wiper:
1) Set the windshield wiper switch to LOW.
NOTE: In the LOW position, the wiper blade moves at approximately 160 strokes
per minute.
2) Make sure that the operation of the wiper is smooth and it clears the water from the
windshield.
NOTE: One stroke = one wipe.
(d) Do these steps to test the high speed operation of the wiper:
1) Set the windshield wiper switch to HIGH.
NOTE: In the HIGH position, the wiper blade moves at approximately 250 strokes
per minute.
2) Make sure that the operation of the wiper is smooth and it clears the water from the
windshield.
NOTE: One stroke = one wipe.
(e) Set the windshield wiper switch to LOW.
1) Make sure that the wipers go to low speed operation.
(f) Set the windshield wiper switch to INT .
1) Make sure that the wipers go to intermittent operation.
(g) Set the windshield wiper switch to PARK.
1) Make sure that the wiper blades park near the lower edge of the glass.
(h) Stop the water spray to the window.
D. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition

SUBTASK 30-42-11-010-003
CAUTION
DO NOT LIFT THE ARM ASSEMBLY MORE THAN NECESSARY TO LIFT THE
ARM OFF THE WINDSHIELD. YOU CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE ARM
ASSEMBLY AND MAKE IT UNSERVICEABLE.

SUBTASK 30-42-11-200-001
(2) Check the tension and general condition of the wiper blade [1].
(a) If the wiper blade [1] is damaged or worn, replace the wiper blade [1]
(TASK 30-42-11-020-801 and TASK 30-42-11-400-801).
(b) If it is necessary, adjust the blade tension adjustment nut [3] to get a wiper arm tension of
6.5 lbf (28.9 N) - 7.5 lbf (33.4 N).
SUBTASK 30-42-11-210-001
(3) Make sure that the part mark is near the inside edge of the window seal (away from pilot and

INSTRUCTION, DAMAGE TO THE AIRPLANE SKIN CAN OCCUR.

(a) If the blade discs are damaged or worn, replace the wiper blade [1]
(TASK 30-42-11-020-801 and TASK 30-42-11-400-801).
(b) If the wiper arm disc is damaged or worn, replace the wiper arm (TASK 30-42-31-000-801

BLADE TO MOVE AND TURN. DAMAGE TO AIRCRAFT SKIN AND THE
WINDSHIELD STRUCTURE CAN OCCUR.
(5) Make sure that the wiper blade [1] is tightly attached to the wiper arm.
(a) If it is necessary, tighten the nut [2] to 18 in-lb (2.0 N·m) - 20 in-lb (2.3 N·m).
SUBTASK 30-42-11-160-001
WARNING
USE EYE PROTECTION WHEN YOU USE COMPRESSED AIR TO CLEAN,
COOL, OR DRY PARTS OR TOOLS. PARTICLES CAN CAUSE AN INJURY TO
YOUR EYES. DO NOT POINT COMPRESSED AIR AT YOURSELF OR OTHER
PERSONS.
(6) If it is necessary, clean the wiper blade [1] with a lint-free cloth, G01043, or with compressed
air.

(1) Put the wiper motor converter [1] into position.
SUBTASK 30-42-21-400-001
(2) Install the screws [2] and washers [3].
SUBTASK 30-42-21-100-002
(3) Clean the ground wire terminal mating surface and the ground stud on the airplane structure.
(a) Use a cotton wiper, G00034, moist with alcohol, B00130.
(b) Use dry cotton wiper, G00034, to remove remaining solvent.
30-42-21
SUBTASK 30-42-21-420-001
(4) Connect the ground wire [7] to the airplane structure.
(a) Install the washer [5] and nut [4].
(b) Make sure that the ground wire [7] does not contact surrounding components.
SUBTASK 30-42-21-760-002
(5) Measure the electrical bonding resistance between the ground wire [7] and airplane structure
(SWPM 20-20-00).
(a) Use the non-intrinsically safe bonding meter, COM-614.
(b) Make sure that the electrical bonding resistance is 0.001 ohm (1.0 milliohm) or less.
SUBTASK 30-42-21-420-002
(6) Connect the electrical connector [6] to the wiper motor converter [1].
H. Wiper Motor/Converter Installation Test
SUBTASK 30-42-21-710-001
(1) Do these steps to make sure that the wiper motor converter [1] operates:
(a) Do this task: Supply External Power, TASK 24-22-00-860-813.
(b) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
B 1 C00055 ANTI-ICE & RAIN WSHLD WIPER RIGHT
B 3 C00054 ANTI-ICE & RAIN WSHLD WIPER LEFT
(c) Set the windshield wiper switch to LOW.
(d) Make sure that the wiper motor converter [1] operates.
(e) Turn the windshield wiper switch to PARK.
SUBTASK 30-42-21-410-002
(2) Re-install the P1-3 panel for Captain side and P3-1 panel for F/O side.
SUBTASK 30-42-21-410-001
(3) Re-install the display unit (TASK 31-62-11-400-801).
SUBTASK 30-42-21-390-001
(4) Apply sealant, A00247, to the wiper motor shaft.
(a) Make sure that the area around the wiper motor shaft is clean (TASK 51-31-00-160-801).
(b) Use a sealant gun, STD-7413, to fill the gap between the wiper motor shaft and the
airplane skin with sealant, A00247.
1) Apply sealant, A00247, completely around the wiper motor shaft.
2) Make sure that all gaps are completely filled.
(c) Use a hardwood or plastic fillet smoothing spatula, STD-810, to make a smooth surface
on the sealant.
(d) Remove all excess sealant with a clean cotton wiper, G00034, that is wet with Series 92
solvent, B01012 (TASK 20-30-92-910-801).
SUBTASK 30-42-21-420-003
(5) Re-install the windshield wiper arm (TASK 30-42-31-400-801).
30-42-21
SUBTASK 30-42-21-410-003
(6) If you must send the airplane before the sealant is fully cured, install 3M 425 Aluminum Foil
T ape, G00291 on top of the sealant.
(a) Remove the 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape, G00291 when the sealant is fully cured.
NOTE: The use of 3M 425 Aluminum Foil T ape, G00291 is not intended for long term use
but only until the sealant is cured.

(2) Apply grease, D00013, to the serrations on the adjustment sleeve [10] and motor-converter
shaft.
SUBTASK 30-42-31-420-007
(3) Install the arm assembly [3] on the motor-converter shaft.
(a) Place the arm assembly [3] on the motor-converter shaft in the parked position.
NOTE: To determine the position of the wiper arm assembly and for adjustment
instructions refer to Figure 201.
1) For large adjustments, turn the arm assembly [3] and adjustment sleeve [10]
together on the motor-converter shaft.
2) For small adjustments, turn the adjustment sleeve [10] within the arm assembly [3].
NOTE: For fine adjustments of the arm assembly, remove the adjustment sleeve
from the arm and turn the sleeve in the same direction that the arm must be
moved. Each notch moves the wiper end about 0.034 in. (0.864 mm).
30-42-31
(b) Apply 3.5 lb (1.6 kg) to 4.5 lb (2.0 kg) downward force at the blade attach point.
1) Make sure that the blade assembly [9] stays on the glass.
SUBTASK 30-42-31-420-008
(4) Install the bolt [5].
(a) Make sure that the head of the bolt [5] is on the same side as the lockwire hole in the
wiper arm.
(b) Tighten the bolt [5] to hold the arm assembly [3] and adjustment sleeve [10] in their
positions.
1) Make sure that the arm assembly [3] and the adjustment sleeve [10] are above the
end of the motor-converter shaft.
SUBTASK 30-42-31-420-009
(5) Install the washer [8] and bolt [7] on the end of the motor-converter shaft.
SUBTASK 30-42-31-420-013
(6) Tighten the bolt [7] to 20 in-lb (2.3 N·m) – 25 in-lb (2.8 N·m).
SUBTASK 30-42-31-420-014
(7) Make sure that the adjustment sleeve [10] and arm assembly [3] touch the bolt [7] and
washer [8] (View A-A, Figure 201).
(a) Make sure that there is no clearance.
SUBTASK 30-42-31-420-012
(8) Tighten the bolt [5] to 45 in-lb (5.1 N·m) – 50 in-lb (5.6 N·m).
SUBTASK 30-42-31-420-006
(9) If it is removed, do this task: Windshield Wiper Blade Installation, TASK 30-42-11-400-801.
SUBTASK 30-42-31-820-011
(10) Do this task: Windshield Wiper Arm Force - Check/Adjustment, TASK 30-42-31-820-801.
SUBTASK 30-42-31-820-012
(11) Do this task: Windshield Wiper Arm Position - Check/Adjustment, TASK 30-42-31-820-802.
SUBTASK 30-42-31-420-011
(12) Install the lockwire [6] through the hole in the bolt [5], hole in the arm assembly [3], and hole in
the bolt [7] to secure the bolt [5] and bolt [7].
(a) Use MS20995C32 lockwire, G01048 (preferred), or cable assembly, SPL-22466
(alternative), with a double twist method.

WINDSHIELD.
CAUTION

NOT OBEY , IT CAN CAUSE MARKS ON THE COATED GLASS SURFACE.
(b) Supply a continuous spray of clean water to the windshield.

30-42-31
1) If water with a high mineral content is used on a windshield with a hydrophobic

(3) Do these steps to measure the force of the wiper arm:
(a) Connect the spring scale, STD-759, at the point where the blade assembly [9] attaches to

CAUTION
DO NOT LIFT THE WIPER ARM HIGHER THAN NECESSARY . DAMAGE
TO THE WIPER ARM CAN OCCUR IF YOU LIFT IT TOO HIGH.
(c) Pull the spring scale, STD-759, slowly until the blade assembly [9] lifts off the windshield
to measure the force.
NOTE: One end of the wiper blade may remain on the windshield during this test. A
piece of paper should be able to slide under the blade without binding.
SUBTASK 30-42-31-820-006
(4) Adjust the force of the arm assembly [3].
NOTE: The wiper motor has a dimple at the end of the motor/converter shaft.
(a) If the force is less than 6.5 lbf (28.9 N), tighten the adjustment screw [4] to increase the
force of the arm assembly [3].
(b) If the force is more than 7.5 lbf (33.4 N), loosen the adjustment screw [4] to decrease the
force of the arm assembly [3].
NOTE: The wiper motor is to be tensioned to the specified tension above, to be in compliance
with AD 2003-20-13.
SUBTASK 30-42-31-700-002

arm assembly [3] again and adjust as required.

(3) Do these steps to examine the sweep pattern of the arm assembly [3]:
CAUTION
DO NOT OPERATE THE WINDSHIELD WIPERS ON DRY GLASS.

OPERATE THE WIPER. THE WIPER WILL CAUSE DAMAGE TO A DRY
WINDSHIELD.
(a) Supply a continuous spray of clean water to the windshield.
NOTE: For windshields with a hydrophobic coating, deionized or distilled water is
recommended during the wiper test. Water with a high mineral content can cause
marks on the coated glass surface that are difficult to remove. If water with a high
mineral content is used on a windshield with a hydrophobic coating, flush the
windshield surface after the wiper test with deionized or distilled water, or remove
the remaining water on the windshield with a cotton cloth.

assembly [9] near the bottom edge of the windshield.
(f) Make sure that the blade assembly [9] is parallel to the bottom edge of the windshield.
30-42-31

installation.
(c) Remove the windshield arm assembly [3] (TASK 30-42-31-000-801).
(d) Re-install the windshield arm assembly [3] (TASK 30-42-31-400-801).

assembly.

WINDSHIELD.

recommended during the wiper test. Water with a high mineral content can cause
marks on the coated glass surface that are difficult to remove. If water with a high
mineral content is used on a windshield with a hydrophobic coating, flush the
windshield surface after the wiper test with deionized or distilled water, or remove
the remaining water on the windshield with a cotton cloth.
(b) Set the wiper switch to the LOW position.
(c) Set the wiper switch to the PARK position.
(d) Stop the water spray.

30-43-00-800-801Hydrophobic Coating - Maintenance PracticesPDF p. 385
Open original AMM PDF - page 385 ↗ 
2. Hydrophobic Coating - Maintenance Practices
A. General
(1) The maintenance procedures for the hydrophobic coating are contained in the Master Kit listed
below.
(2) The windshield wipers are the certified rain removal system. The hydrophobic coating is an
enhancement and is not required for dispatch. However, Boeing recommends that the coating
be maintained.
B. Tools/Equipment
NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the
tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers
that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional.

30-71-00-800-801Water and Drain Operation with 115 Volts on the GroundPDF p. 389
Open original AMM PDF - page 389 ↗ 
2. Water and Drain Operation with 115 Volts on the Ground
A. General
(1) 28 volts AC is normally used to provide protection from icing for the water and drain system
while the airplane is on the ground. 115 volt AC is used while the airplane is in the air.
(2) This task should only be used when the 28 volt operation will not remove ice from the drain
masts.
(3) Refer to Cold Weather Maintenance Procedure, TASK 12-33-01-600-802.
B. References
Reference Title
12-33-01-600-802 Cold Weather Maintenance Procedure (P/B 301)

32-09-00-860-801 Put the Airplane in the Air Mode (P/B 201)
32-09-00-860-802 Return the Airplane to the Ground Mode (P/B 201)

EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR.

WARNING

MASTS BECOME VERY HOT . INJURIES TO PERSONNEL CAN OCCUR.
(2) Make sure that the applicable drain heater does not become warm.
(a) You can use an Infrared Thermometer, COM-1572, or other temperature measuring
device, to make sure that the temperature is less than 10 degrees F (6 degrees C)
warmer than the ambient air temperature.
(b) As an alternative, you can use a clamp-on current meter, COM-2531, or equivalent, to
measure the current to the drain heater.
1) Make sure that there is no current to the drain heater.

details to get access to the heater wiring.

E. Check the Ground Mode Operation of the Drain Mast Heaters
SUBTASK 30-71-00-720-001
WARNING
DO NOT TOUCH THE DRAIN MAST . WHEN THE DRAIN MAST HEATER
OPERATES, THE DRAIN MAST IS SUFFICIENTLY HOT TO BURN YOU.
(1) Make sure that the forward and aft drain masts become warm.
(a) You can use an Infrared Thermometer, COM-1572 or other temperature measuring
device to make sure the drain mast is at least 10 degrees F (6 degrees C) warmer than
the ambient air temperature.
(b) As an alternative, you can use a clamp-on current meter, COM-2531 or equivalent to
measure the current to the drain mast heater.
1) Current to the drain mast indicates that the heater is operating.
2) Refer to (TASK 38-31-01-000-801) for details to access the heater wiring.
SUBTASK 30-71-00-860-003
WARNING
YOU MUST DO THE STEPS IN THE TASK BELOW TO PREPARE THE
SAFETY-SENSITIVE SYSTEMS FOR THE AIR MODE. FAILURE TO DO THE
STEPS CORRECTLY WILL CAUSE THE AUTOMATIC OPERATION OF
AIRPLANE SYSTEMS. THIS CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONS OR
DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT .
(2) Do this task: Prepare to Put the Airplane in the Air Mode, TASK 32-09-00-840-801.
SUBTASK 30-71-00-860-004
(3) Put the airplane in the air mode. To put it in the air mode, do this task: Put the Airplane in the
Air Mode, TASK 32-09-00-860-801.
SUBTASK 30-71-00-860-005
WARNING
DO NOT TOUCH THE DRAIN MAST . WHEN THE DRAIN MAST HEATER
OPERATES, THE DRAIN MAST IS SUFFICIENTLY HOT TO BURN YOU.
CAUTION
DO NOT OPERATE THE DRAIN MAST HEATERS IN THE AIR MODE FOR
MORE THAN 5 MINUTES. THE DRAIN MASTS WILL BECOME TOO HOT .
(4) Make sure that this circuit breaker is closed:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
E 4 C00700 HEATERS DRAIN MAST AIR
30-71-00
SUBTASK 30-71-00-720-002
(5) Make sure that the forward and aft drain masts become hot.
(a) You can use an Infrared Thermometer, COM-1572 or other temperature measuring
device to make sure the drain mast is at least 10 degrees F (6 degrees C) warmer than
the ground mode test temperature.
(b) As an alternative, you can use a clamp-on current meter, COM-2531 or equivalent to
measure the current to the drain mast heater.
1) Make sure there is greater current to the heater than in the ground mode.

6) Do this task: Carpet Removal, TASK 25-27-15-000-801.
7) Do this task: Entry and Service Area Floor Covering - Removal,
TASK 25-27-21-000-801.
SUBTASK 30-71-00-720-003
(2) If the heater is controlled by a thermostat apply the MS-242N spray, G02319, ice, G02320, or
dry ice to decrease the temperature of the thermostat, (WDM 30-71-11).
SUBTASK 30-71-00-720-004
(3) Make sure the heater gets warm.
(a) You can use a Infrared Thermometer, COM-1572 or other temperature measuring device
to make sure there is an increase of at least 10 degrees F (6 degrees C) above the
ambient air temperature.
(b) As an alternative, you can use a clamp-on current meter, COM-2531 or equivalent to
measure the current to the heater.
(c) Current to the heater indicates that the heater is operating.
G. Put the Airplane Back to its Usual Condition
SUBTASK 30-71-00-410-002
(1) Replace or close the panels or doors used to access the heater and thermostat.
(a) Do this task: Cargo Floor Panel - Installation, TASK 25-52-10-400-801.
(b) Do this task: Forward Cargo Compartment Aft Bulkhead Liner - Installation,
TASK 25-52-17-400-801.
(c) Do this task: Aft Cargo Compartment Aft Bulkhead Liner - Installation,
TASK 25-52-19-400-801.
(d) Do this task: Cargo Compartment Sidewall Lining - Installation, TASK 25-52-06-400-801.
(e) Do this task: Waste T ank Enclosure Panel - Installation, TASK 25-52-20-400-801.

F . Waste Tank Rinse Fitting Heater Installation
SUBTASK 30-71-04-420-001
(1) Install the rinse fitting heater [2] on the service panel rinse fitting.
(a) Torque the rinse fitting heater [2] to 300 in-lb (34 N·m) to 350 in-lb (40 N·m).
SUBTASK 30-71-04-160-001
(2) Prepare the pan surface for attaching the rinse fitting heater thermostat [3].
(a) Remove all remaining adhesive.
(b) Wipe the pan surface with solvent, B00083.
SUBTASK 30-71-04-390-001
(3) Install the rinse rinse fitting heater thermostat [3] to the pan surface.
NOTE: Make sure that you do not install the rinse fitting heater thermostat on a rivet or other
fastener.
(a) If the temperature is less than 65°F (18°C), heat the pan surface.
NOTE: At 65°F (18°C), the handling time for the adhesive is 3 hours, full cure time is 24
hours. At 140°F (60°C)(maximum), the handling time is 20 minutes, full cure time
is 2 hours.
30-71-04
(b) Attach the rinse fitting heater thermostat [3] to the pan surface with adhesive, A00870,
any class.
SUBTASK 30-71-04-420-002
(4) Attach the rinse hose [1] to the rinse fitting heater [2].
(a) Torque the rinse hose connect to 300 in-lb (34 N·m) to 350 in-lb (40 N·m).
SUBTASK 30-71-04-420-003
CAUTION
AVOID KINKING OR SHARPLY BENDING HARDWIRE LEAD AS DAMAGE TO
TRANSDUCER AND LEAD CAN OCCUR.
(5) Attach splices to the rinse fitting heater [2] wiring.
SUBTASK 30-71-04-860-001
(6) Remove the safety tag and close this circuit breaker:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
D 18 C01463 WASTE/WTR LINE HEATERS
G. Waste Tank Rinse Fitting Heater Installation Test
SUBTASK 30-71-04-610-002
(1) Cool the rinse fitting heater thermostat with ice, G02320, MS-242N spray, G02319, or
compound, G01012.
SUBTASK 30-71-04-610-003
(2) Make sure that the rinse fitting heater [2] becomes warm.
SUBTASK 30-71-04-170-001
(3) Fill the waste tank with 30 gal (114 l) to 40 gal (151 l) of water.
SUBTASK 30-71-04-790-001
(4) Examine the rinse fitting assembly and the connections for leakage.

2. Heated Potable Water Hose Removal
(Figure 401)
A. General
(1) This task gives instructions to remove the Heated Potable Water Hose.
(2) The procedures to remove the hoses are similar. Access to the hoses is either through the aft
cargo compartment or through the floor near the aft lavatory.

G. Heated Potable Water Hose Installation
SUBTASK 30-71-06-420-001
(1) Connect the heated potable water hose fittings.
(a) For the fill fitting, tighten the heated hose to 620 ±80 in-lb (70 ±9 N·m).
(b) For all other fittings, refer to the task: Flexible Hose Installation, TASK 20-10-52-400-801.
SUBTASK 30-71-06-420-002
(2) Connect the electrical connectors to the heated water hose.
H. Heated Potable Water Hose Installation Test
SUBTASK 30-71-06-790-001
(1) Do a test for leaks in the potable water hose (TASK 38-10-00-790-801).
SUBTASK 30-71-06-720-001
(2) Measure the temperature of the hose and surrounding area.
SUBTASK 30-71-06-860-003
(3) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers:
CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3
Row Col Number Name
D 18 C01463 WASTE/WTR LINE HEATERS
E 5 C00233 HEATERS DRAIN
RYR 378-999
E 18 C01473 HOSE HEATERS

132, 135, 138-169, 174-179, 182-187, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236, 239-298, 301-304,
306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-377
F 16 C01473 HOSE HEATERS
RYR ALL
SUBTASK 30-71-06-720-002
(4) Do this test of the heated hose:
(a) If the temperature in the area is higher than 45 degrees F (7 degrees C), then decrease
the temperature of the thermostat.
NOTE: You can use MS-242N spray, G02319, ice, G02320, or compound, G01012 to
decrease the temperature. Permit 3 to 5 minutes for the temperature of the
thermostat to decrease before you check the temperature of the heated hose.
The thermostat is in a bulge at the end of the hose.
(b) Make sure the hose heater is powered by either of these methods:
1) Use a Infrared Thermometer, COM-1572, or equivalent, to make sure the hose gets
warm.
30-71-06
2) Use a clamp-on current meter, COM-2531,or equivalent, to make sure that the
heater is drawing electrical current.
